Draggable, the default, a hold anywhere on the card (`HOLD_MS`, 150 ms, for a mouse or a pen; `TOUCH_HOLD_MS`, 300 ms, for a finger, the same as every hold to drag in the app (`holdMs`); with the pointer still within `HOLD_TOLERANCE_PX`; a mouse may also pull `HOLD_DISTANCE_PX` at once; a finger that lets go before, without a move, taps) picks the note up — the card presses down while the hold runs (`.card-held`) and lifts and tilts as it is picked up (`.card-drag-overlay`, the `card-lift` keyframes) — and from then on it follows the pointer. A press that ends first is a click, and the control under it still gets it; a press that drifts first is a scroll or a text selection. A press on an input, a text field, or a control marked `data-no-drag` never drags. Editing, the pencil's mode, keeps the press in its fields, its bar, and its buttons (`data-no-drag`), and a hold on the header row still picks the note up — to reorder it, to move it, or to hold it over another note until the ring closes; its draft is saved before a merge reads it (below); a viewer's notes never drag. There is no grip on a note (a section keeps its grip). A search no longer pauses the drag: the drop's place is counted in the section's whole list either way. A drag that changes nothing — let go where it was, off every list, or Escape — scrolls the lists back to where they were when the card lifted (`restoreLiftScroll`). The drop line, in a column and in a grid of tiles, carries a knob at its start, as the document list's does. A move draws again only the cards the line and the ring leave and reach (the line and the covered card are stores each card reads, `useLine`, `useIsMergeTarget`). Nothing in a list moves while a drag runs — the card rides in a drag overlay under the pointer, its place left dimmed, and a clay line in the gap says where it would land: on the top half of a card the line goes above it, on the bottom half below it, and a list holding nothing takes the drop on its own space. A section's title row takes the drop too (`data-drop-header`): it lights, the line stands above the section's first note, and the note lands at the top of the section — a folded section in the tray as well, which then unfolds and shows the note, as a note moved to another section always is. A quote or an annotation held over a title row says where it lands on the row itself, in place of the section's actions, so the notes under it stay where they are. Escape while a note is lifted puts it back and does nothing else: the drag hears Escape first and keeps it from the board, the selection, and every other listener. Cards that slid out from under the pointer are what made the old preview flicker between a reorder and a merge; a line over cards that hold still says the same thing and never moves the target. One drag crosses sections — the whole surface is one board (`SortableBoard`, `SortableGroup` in sortable.tsx), so a note dropped in another section's notes moves there, at the place the line stood. Moving a note between sections is a main action, so it is the drag itself: there is no "Move to…" menu on a note. The drop's place comes from the note the line stood before, not from its index in the list (`dropIndex` in board-lists.ts): a list a composer owns a note in shows one note fewer than the section holds, and a note moving down its own list leaves its own place before it lands. A board holding lists of two kinds says which take which (`canDrop`), so a section drag never draws a line among notes. The notes full page adds section reorder, renaming, compare, and export.
- The notes full page is one press away from the tray: the four-arrows button (`MaximizeIcon`) at the end of the tray's one row before the notes — the search, Expand all, the view menu, the four arrows — opens it, in place of the old bar under the notes. The view menu (`NotesViewMenu`, `note-groups.tsx`) holds which notes show (All notes, This document) and Group by; its button reads pressed, with a dot, while the tray shows This document alone. A project with no note yet shows no row and no count beside Notes: every control on the row acts on notes, and the empty state is its one line. The tray stays mounted, hidden, while another tab shows, once it has shown: a return to Notes draws no card anew. On the notes full page a click on a section's title opens the section's **board** (`section-board.tsx`): the section's notes filling the screen as tiles side by side, one grid (`.note-board`; the columns are as many 220px tiles as the board's width holds — 150px tiles on a board narrower than 500px, so a phone shows two columns — and no more than the notes, so few notes take wide tiles, up to 480px; `NoteTile`), each tile the note's id, its title, and its body. A tile shows its whole body when the board has the room: the board's height, less the composer and the gaps, is shared among the rows of tiles, and a tile grows to its share (`--tile-max`), never under the 3:4 tile — taller than wide — (`--tile-min`); the tiles of one row stretch to one height. A body the tile cannot hold whole is cut and fades out at the bottom (`.note-tile-cut`); a body shown whole reads to its last line. A board of many notes keeps its 3:4 tiles. A tile is in the note's draggable mode and nothing else: a hold anywhere picks it up, the line stands between tiles (a grid list, `layout="grid"` on `SortableGroup`: the left half of a tile lands the drag before it, the right half after it), and a tile held over another until the ring closes joins it. A click on a tile opens the note whole over the board — its card, with the pencil, the jump, the history — and ✕, a click beside it, or Esc closes it; editing happens there, never in a tile. The board's header carries the section's own actions (+ Note, Command), the sections inside it as chips (each opens its board), the section it sits in (one press up), and ✕ or Esc to close. The board takes the focus as it opens, so the first Tab lands on its own controls, and the page behind it is inert; closed, it gives the focus back to the section's title. A note rejected on the board shows its Undo pill over the board. The section's pencil, beside its title, renames it. The board draws its first 12 tiles as it opens and 12 more each frame after.
- Merge is a hold, not a pass, and it reads the cards, not the pointer: once the dragged note covers more than 80% of another note (`MERGE_COVER`, of the smaller of the two — a one-line note dropped on a long one covers little of it and all of itself, and either way it is over that note) a ring draws itself around the covered note over one second (`MERGE_DWELL_MS`), and at the full ring the merge runs. There is nothing to press: the hold is the whole gesture, and the ring is the reader watching it commit. Moving on before the ring closes puts the line back. A drag that passes over a note on its way somewhere else never merges anything. The pointer is not the card: a note picked up by its grip hangs below and right of the pointer, so a pointer on a note means the dragged note is somewhere above it — reading the pointer merged the wrong note, or none. What the reader sees is one note covering another, so that is what decides. The dragged note draws back a little while it covers one, so the ring underneath shows on all four sides. The floating card over the article is one more card a hold can merge into (`[data-floating-note]`); it takes the drop itself, its own words saved first. A note let go off every list and over an open document leaves the list: the board reports the drop (`onDropOutside`) and the tray floats the note where the card was seen, in its draggable mode.
- What the merge looks like (globals.css, `.merge-ring` / `.merge-fall` / `.note-absorb` / `.note-merging` / `.note-merged`): the ring closes, the held note falls into the note it merges into — landing on where that note ends up, not where it was, since the merge takes the held note out of the list and moves the other up a row — and that note blooms as it takes it in. The joined text is there at once and settles in. Merge with AI (the ticker's bulk action) works after the bloom: the note keeps its row, its controls stand down, and its id sits beside the spark and the sheened label every AI tool in the app uses (`ThinkingIndicator`), while a sheen crosses the card and the model writes the note that replaces both. Reduced motion keeps all of it still — the ring stands drawn, the fall is a fade, and the words carry the state.
- A merge is a join (`lib/notes/join.ts`; `POST /api/notes/merge {mode: "join"}`, the default): the notes' text lands in one note as it is, in the order the notes stand in — the note on top first, the note under it after, whichever was dragged; the route reads the order from the sections and the rows, the client's optimistic join from the tree. The first note's title is the merged note's title; a later note's title stays in the body as a heading over that note's text (`## `). A hold, a drop on the floating card, an annotation dropped on a note, and the ticker's Merge all join. Merge with AI stays on the ticker, beside Merge: the model writes the one note that takes their place — a title, then one section per key point (a level-two heading that states the point, one to three sentences in the reader's own words), with every quote of the notes placed verbatim under the point it supports; a point in only one note stays, a point in several notes is written once, and every image, link, and checklist item stays (`lib/prompts/merge.ts`, `lib/notes/merge.ts`; `mode: "ai"`). The route checks the quotes after the call — a quote the model dropped or rewrote is added at the end as it was (`withEveryQuote`), since the quotes are what the sources point back to; a call that fails joins instead. Either way the sources' anchors and replies move to the target, the sources are deleted, the target's gist is cleared, and the merge is an edit in the target's history. Accepted notes only — pending notes go through Accept/Reject first.
- A merge can be undone: what it took apart — the target's text before, and every consumed note with its anchors and replies — is kept as the meta of a `NOTE_MERGE` history event (`lib/notes/merge-snapshot.ts`), and the answer carries the event's id. A pill under the notes offers Undo for twelve seconds after each merge (`MergeUndoBar`; the next merge or delete takes the pill). Every pill is the same: what went, Undo, ✕, twelve seconds, Ctrl+Z presses Undo — a merge, a delete, a reject, an edit canceled, a section deleted, and a delete posted from outside the notes. On a touch screen its Undo and ✕ are 36 px tall. A delete of an annotation or a comment (the Annotations tab, the annotations full page, the reader's cards — a highlight's, a comment's, and the Explain, Analyze, Visualize, and Simplify cards' trash — the page editor's comment card) asks nothing and posts to the pill (`dissect:undo-pill`, `lib/notes/undo-pill.ts`: `{ message, undo, commit? }`; `deleteNoteWithUndo`): "Comment deleted", "Highlight deleted", "Annotation deleted", or "Conversation deleted", and Undo is History's Restore of that delete. Undo puts the notes back on screen at once, as they stood, when the undo will run (the merged note holds what the merge wrote and no editor is open on it); else the server's answer decides, and nothing flickers. Once the merged note's text changes, the pill reads "Edited since the merge" in place of Undo. A refused undo says why in the pill and is not a failed save: the header does not turn to Not saved. With the focus outside a text box, Ctrl+Z (⌘Z) presses the Undo of the pill on screen. The pill and the selection bar are drawn on the page's body (`onBody`, merge-undo.tsx), never inside the tray's scroll box, so they stay at the bottom of the screen however far the tray is scrolled. `POST /api/notes/merge/undo {undoId}` puts the target's text back, re-creates the consumed notes with their ids at their rows, moves their anchors and replies back, and deletes the anchors copied from annotations — while the target still holds the merged text; a note edited since is the reader's newer work, and the undo refuses. A merge is undone once. The History panel lists the merge ("merged notes into one").
- A full page load keeps the reader where it was (a note, an annotation, or an AI tool refreshes the page, and a new deploy or a dropped response turns the next refresh into a full load), and a document opens where the account left off in it — in a new tab, on another device, another day. The **reading position** is the block at the reading line (80px under the pane's top edge, below the controls that float there; in the page editor 32px under its header, the title row and the toolbar, so the block and its left-off mark show — `readingLine`) and the offset from the line to the block's top, not a pixel count, so a figure above it that loads late moves nothing (`lib/reading-position.ts`). It saves twice: the tab's copy in sessionStorage as the reader scrolls, beside the drawer's state — the reader's own open or fold and the tab, saved when the reader makes them (the rail, a jump to a note or an annotation, Show all comments), never a fold the workspace makes by itself (a floating note, a side chat, a blank document's default, §29); and the account's copy, one row per account per document (`ReadingPosition`, `PUT /api/documents/[documentId]/position`, viewer), 3 seconds after the reader stops scrolling, at least every 15 seconds while they keep scrolling, and at once when the tab hides, the page goes, or the document closes (a save sent as the page goes can be lost — a reload drops it — so the copy is kept current before then) — fire and forget, never in the save indicator, and never a project write (no rev, no history). Each copy carries when the reader was there, by the reading browser's clock; the row changes only for a newer save, and on open the newer copy wins, the tab's on a tie. Only the reader's own scrolls count as moves: not the hold's, not a page opened over the article, not a tab out of sight. The tab's copy restores exactly; the account's copy resumes: the block's top lands on the reading line, unless the reader was so deep in a long block that the line they were on would land below 60% of the pane — then it restores exactly, the offset scaled to the block's height on this screen. An inline script applies the position and the drawer's state, or folds the tray for a blank document, before the first paint (the account's copy rides on the pane, `data-account-position`), and the reader holds it while the layout under it settles, until the reader scrolls. A transcript keeps the tab's copy alone (playback moves its pane); an embedded layer keeps none. A `?src`, `?block`, or `?link` jump wins over the restore. Once the reader moves on from the jump — a wheel or a touch drag in the panes, a scrolling key — or 30 seconds after it, the address drops `?src`, `?block`, `?link`, and `?annotation` (`components/reader/jump-param.ts`), so a reload opens the document at the reading position, not back at the jump.
- **The left-off mark** (`LeftOffMark`, reader.tsx): a small clay ribbon in the left margin and a faint line across the column, in the gap above the block of the reading position the document opened with, so the place still shows once the reader scrolls on or away; its tooltip reads "You left off here". It stays put for the visit (a refresh moves nothing), shows in reading mode only, and shows under a jump too, so the reader can scroll back to the place; a position still on the document's first screen (less than a third of the pane down) has none. It sits outside the block's hover group, so pointing at it leaves the grip hidden, and it takes no room and is no part of the block's text. The block reader follows the page editor's rule: when the exact restore of the tab's copy leaves the block's top above the pane's edge, the mark stands above the first block whose top shows; a block taller than the pane keeps it. In the page editor (a blank document, an import) the same mark is a widget at the block's start (`layer/left-off.ts`), drawn over the page and never written into the rich text, in every mode; it moves with its block as the text changes and never prints. It shows when the document opens: when the exact restore of the tab's copy leaves the block's top under the header (the reader was past its first lines), the mark stands above the first block whose top shows under the header, and a block that runs past the pane's bottom has it inside, at the reading line; the scroll is restored as it was.
- **First open.** A document opened right after it was added reveals as the reader scrolls, as if laid out in sequence (`reveal.tsx`): the add sets `unitos-reveal:<documentId>` in sessionStorage before the open (document-bar.tsx), and the reader takes the flag on mount. Every block wrapper starts invisible in its place; one IntersectionObserver on the pane's scroll box gives each block its turn as it enters the pane — blocks in view together in document order, 110 ms apart — and a text block unclips line by line (`steps(n)`, one step per line at 70 ms, 1.4 s at most) while a figure, a table, or a page fades in and rises 8px over 320 ms. A revealed block stays revealed: the wrapper keeps no class and no style after its turn, and it has no padding, border, or overflow, so margins collapse through it as before. Reading mode only — never the transcript, never over a reading position restore or a `?src`, `?block`, or `?link` jump, never under reduced motion, and never on a full page load, where the article is already painted.
- Note edits auto-save: while a note's editor is open, every edit saves on its own — a debounced PATCH after the last keystroke, a keepalive flush when the window closes — so nothing typed is lost. Done closes the editor; Cancel and Esc restore the content from before this edit, auto-saves included. Every keystroke is also written to localStorage (`lib/note-drafts.ts`, `unitos-note-draft:<noteId>`): the PATCH and the flush are network calls, and the local draft is what survives a crash, a power loss, or a lost connection between them. It is cleared when the server confirms the same content — a save queued offline is not a confirmation (§17) — and a draft names the account that wrote it, so a page signed in as another account neither shows nor sends it; on the next load, `useOutline` checks each remaining draft against its note — the same content clears it; anything else is written to the note, made from the text the draft was made from (a draft written before drafts kept that text is cleared when the note changed elsewhere since, `updatedAt` later than the draft). While that save has not landed, the card shows the draft's words, marked Not saved, never the note's old text, and the editor opens on them, saving them made from the text they were made from; no keystroke writes the local draft before the editor shows the kept words. While the page stays open, the save is tried again when the network comes back, when the tab is shown again, and every 20 seconds, for every draft whose note no editor holds open; the Not saved mark clears with the save. A note save on its way is drawn over the server's copy until it answers, so a refresh (another document opened) never shows the old text meanwhile. **Words written to a gone note are kept** (`lib/notes/gone.ts`): when the note was deleted or merged away elsewhere, or its section was deleted — another tab, a collaborator, while an edit waited in the offline queue — the first write with words makes a new note of them in its section at its place (else the section of the same title, else the project's first; a project with no section left gets one of the gone section's title), its anchors copied when the text still quotes them, and the history event remembers it (`keptAs` in its meta; a section's event keeps one per note, `keptAsByNote`). A section's closing flush that adds no words to what its event kept makes no note: the section's Undo or Restore brings the note back. A composer's create sent to a deleted section lands in that same section (`sectionAfterRemoval`), and every later write and a delete of the gone note go to that note. On load, a draft with words whose note is not in the project's notes is sent with `onlyIfGone`: a gone note's words become that new note, and a note that still exists (another project's) refuses the write and keeps its draft for its own project's load. The answer carries `keptAs` and the new note's text; the editor moves to the new note with its draft — the card keeps its React key (`noteKey`), so the open editor goes on with every key typed in it — its next save is made from that text, and a stored text that only put in words the reader's text holds and types on from is no conflict. The pill, in the pill's own color (only a failure is red), says "This note was deleted elsewhere. Your words are kept in a new note." A draft whose note is no longer in the project's notes is sent the same way on the next load. A note the reader deleted takes its draft with it. **No save writes over words it did not see** (`lib/notes/save-text.ts`): every save of a note's text names the text it was made from (`baseContent` on `PATCH /api/notes/[noteId]`) — the note's text when the editor opened, then the text each save left; one save at a time per editor. When the note changed since — the same note saved from another tab, or by a collaborator — the route refuses the save with 409 and the stored text, and the editor puts the two together (`lib/notes/conflict.ts`, a line-by-line three-way merge): lines only one side changed take that change, lines both changed the same way stay once, and lines both changed differently are kept twice, the other version first, each under a marker line ("Changed in two places at once. The other version:", "Your version:", "End of the two versions. Keep one, then delete these marker lines."). The editor then shows the text as saved, and the save state reads "Saved · both versions kept". A write that cannot read a 409 — the offline queue, the closing flush — sends `onConflict: "keep"`, and the route puts the texts together itself. A write without `baseContent` saves as it always did. The route reads the note's text, checks the base, and writes in one transaction with the note's row locked (`SELECT … FOR UPDATE`): two writes at once — two quotes appended, an append beside an editor's save, two tabs saving — run one after the other, and the second is built from the text the first left. A merge locks its notes the same way and answers 409 when one of them changed since it read them. A section's composer (a new note) auto-saves the same way (`use-note-compose.ts`, `unitos-note-compose:<sectionId>`): after the last keystroke it creates the note on the server, then saves every later edit to it, and keeps the note out of the section's list while it owns it. Save releases the note; Cancel deletes it; Esc keeps a note with text and drops an empty one; Save on an empty composer is Cancel. Done and Esc close the composer at once, never waiting for the server: the note joins the top of the section's list in the same frame, with the words typed (`placeComposed`, `useOutline`), and its save follows. The local draft keeps the words until the server has them, so a reload before that reopens the composer on them; a save that fails puts them back in the section's composer, marked Not saved, and tries them again by itself — when the network comes back and every 20 seconds, while nothing was typed since — then closes the composer as the Done or Escape asked, the note once in the list (`retryFailed`, use-note-compose.ts). Escape with the focus on Done, Cancel, or a button of the editor's bar closes the composer as Escape in the text does. A + Note pressed while that save is on its way opens an empty composer. When a key opened the composer, its close gives the focus back to the section's + Note; when a press opened it, the focus goes to the page, so a key typed after the close never presses + Note. A second press on + Note while the composer is open puts the caret back in it. A section has one composer, whatever draws it — the tray, the notes full page, a section's board: the same words, one note, and the save after the last keystroke runs whether or not a composer is still on screen. So the words typed on a board's composer stay in the notes full page's composer when the board closes, and + Note on a section opens on the words its local draft holds, never empty over them. Closing the tab, leaving the page, or losing power keeps the note: the next load reopens the composer on the local draft, owning the note it created; a create sent again carries the note's id, so the note is never made twice. The create carries an id chosen in the browser (`lib/notes/client-id.ts`, `id` on `POST /api/notes`), written to the local draft before the create leaves: a reload while the create is on its way reopens the composer on the note it made, and a create sent again with the same id answers with that note (the same account, the same project; else 409), never a second copy. A note being edited is never deleted unless the user presses Cancel on a new note or Delete. The composer sits above the section's notes and the note it makes lands at the top of the section (`top: true` on `POST /api/notes`: order -1, then the orders are made 0..n again); every other way a note is made still lands at the end. "+ note" and Speak stay visible (`section-action.ts`), the same in the tray and on the notes full page: they are the section's main actions, and a control that appears only on hover is not found. Writing a note is the main one, so it is the biggest control in the row — a filled clay pill with a plus and the word Note, never a hairline chip; Speak stays a small pill beside it. A section carries no Add section of its own: one Add section sits at the bottom of the notes full page. The top of every note being edited — the tray card's header, the floating card, a section's composer — carries the save state (`save-state.tsx`): "Saving…" while the draft differs from what the server holds, "Saved" once the server confirmed it, "Not saved" when the last save failed (the next keystroke retries).
- **The default section's title** (`lib/section-title.ts`): a new project's first section, and the section notes land in when none is picked, are stored with the default title in the language of the account that made them ("Notes", "笔记"). Drawn — the tray, the notes full page, a board, the document columns — a stored default reads in the reader's language; any other title reads as typed. The stored title never changes, and the rename field shows it as stored.
- A note has a title and a body (`lib/note-title.ts`): the title is the note's first line when that line is a level-one heading — `# Title` — and the body is everything after it; the note stays one markdown string (`Note.content`), so export, the digest, the prompts, and search read the title as the heading it is, and a note whose first line is anything else has no title. The title renders as one bold row above the body (`.note-title`); a collapsed note shows its title in place of the gist, and a note without one asks for a gist as before. Every editor — the tray card, the notes full page, the floating card, a section's composer — is a title field above the body's editor (`NoteTitleField`, `useNoteParts`): Enter in the title moves to the body, the composer opens in the title, and the saved note is the two joined. A heading typed at the top of the body becomes the title when the editor closes.
- One note structure, everywhere a note renders (the tray, the notes full page, a compare pane): a header row — collapse chevron and the note's id at the left (`#` plus the last six characters of its cuid; click copies it; searching `#k3x9pq` finds the note), edit (the pencil: a filled 28px button with a 16px pencil, the biggest control in the row, since editing is the note's main action), jump, pin, and select at the right (the circle; its tooltip names what the selected notes do together — merge, pin, delete, and on the notes full page compare) — then the body, then the actions. The editor keeps that shape: the header stays, and the note is edited as the document it renders to, so the note looks the same after Done as it did while editing.
- Any number of effects stack on the same words, and no surface shows their markers: the editor paints them live, the rendered note nests them, and the collapsed line strips them. The note's colors and underline are stored as tags (`<clay>`, `<u>`) that react-markdown drops, so the renderer turns them into links its `a` override paints (`components/markdown.tsx`); a link cannot hold another link, so nested tags are flattened first — every run of text is emitted once with all the styles covering it in its href, and a chip or a markdown link inside a tag is left alone, the styles passing over it. Converting one tag at a time nested the links and left the outer one showing as `[word](#dissect-style-clay)`.
- Editing renders live — headings, lists, quotes, bold, italic, underline, colors, ¶ chips, links, images — as markdown underneath (`lib/note-markup.ts` renders the document, `lib/note-doc.ts` reads it back after every edit). Typing "- " or "* " (Shift+8) starts a bulleted list, "+ " a dash list (the same list drawn with a dash for a marker; the renderer reads the marker back from the source, `remarkDashLists` in components/markdown.tsx), "1. " a numbered list, "[ ] " a checklist (stored as GFM task items, `- [ ] ` and `- [x] `; the box ticks by a click in the editor and, in the rendered note, by a click that saves the note without opening the editor), "> " a quote; Enter continues it, Enter on an empty item ends it (at the top level with a blank line before the caret, so the next words save outside the quote or the list), Tab nests an item and Shift+Tab brings it back out, Backspace at the start of a marked line drops the marker (a nested item outdents first; there is no Paragraph button, this is the whole of that action), Cmd+B/I/U and the bar's B/I/U style the selection or what is typed next, and the bar's four colors do the same — a color replaces the color the text had, line by line over any selection, so a selection that crosses styled words or lines still colors (wrapping the raw selection in a tag broke the moment it crossed a marker). Two bars: the tray's editor (and the floating card) carries one bar, the same on every note editor, on one row: Undo, Redo | Line ▾ | B, I, U, Color | Voice typing. Line ▾ names the kind of the line under the caret (¶, H1, H2, H3, •, –, 1., ☐, ") and opens one menu: Text, Heading 1, Heading 2, Heading 3, Bulleted list, Dash list, Numbered list, Checklist, Quote, then Indent and Outdent, then Add an image or GIF, each row with its markdown shortcut; the menu is fixed to the window, under the button or over it when the room is above, and a line style picked with the caret in a line keeps the caret where it was in the line's words. Tab and Shift+Tab indent too. The color button shows the four colors in its place until one is picked. Done, Cancel, and the note's assistant share the row under the text (on the floating card: Done, Cancel, Back to the tray, Assistant); the assistant's panel, opened, takes a row of its own under them. The composer's button reads Done too: the note saves as it is typed. Escape closes an editor keeping the words, as Done does, and so does the floating card's Back to the tray. Cancel puts the note back to its text when the editor opened, and the pill under the notes reads Edit canceled with Undo, which puts the typed words back; the composer's Cancel on a note with words deletes it with the delete's Undo. The bar sits at the top of the editor, the title field under it, then the body: the bar is the editor's own, and the title reads as the first line of what it writes. Every typed shortcut works in both bars. Every tool's tooltip names the tool on its first line and its key or typed shortcut on the second (⌘ on a Mac, Ctrl elsewhere; `outline.tip*`). Each level draws its own marker — disc, circle, square; 1., a., i. — in the editor and in the rendered note alike. The editor nests by two spaces a level whatever the marker; markdown nests by the parent item's content column, so the renderer re-indents the lines to the columns markdown expects (`alignListIndents`, components/markdown.tsx) — without it a nested numbered item came out flat the moment the note was rendered. The editing card grows with the note, capped at the height of the pane it scrolls in — the tray's panel, or the window on the notes full page — and past the cap the text scrolls inside the card while the bar and the buttons stay; opening the editor brings the whole card into view. In the tray a note leaves the tray by the same hold: pick it up and let it go over the article, and the note becomes a floating card there, in its draggable mode (a note being edited drags by its header row, and floats the same way). While a card floats the tray folds and the article column moves to the pane's left edge, out from under the card; both come back when the card docks or closes. For a moment after it lands (`FOLLOW_MS`) the card follows the block of text it was let go over while the column moves, so it stays over the words the reader aimed it at; a hold on the card ends that. The floating card has the note's two modes: draggable, the default, shows the note — its title, its body, its sources — and a hold anywhere on it lifts it (`.note-lifted`) and moves it; the pencil opens its editor — the title field and the body's editor, with the same auto-save — and Done and Cancel return it to the draggable mode where it is. The card resizes from its corner in both modes. Dragged into the sidebar — the right column, the rail and the tray when it is open, at its full height (`inSidebar`); below md the rail's bar and the tray's sheet — and let go, it docks: the note goes back into the tray. The card draws in and rings while it is over the sidebar and says what letting go does (`.note-docking`). Back to the tray docks it too (docking from the editor reopens the tray card's editor on the draft). Wrap text, a toggle on the card remembered per browser (`unitos-note-wrap`): the card joins the article's scroll pane at its spot in the text, scrolls with the text, and the lines flow around it — the card announces the gap it needs (`lib/note-wrap.ts`) and the reader draws it as a pair of float spacers at the article's content top — the one position the card measures against exactly, since a float placed before a block lands at the block's margin edge, not its border edge — on the side of the column the card sits on, 18px clear of the card on every side; when less than 200px of text would remain beside the card, the text skips below it instead. Only paragraphs, headings, and lists flow around the card. A figure, a table, a video, a page, an equation, or a code block is one object that a sliver of column would ruin, so it clears the card and takes its place above or below it, whole — but only a block whose place overlaps the card's height clears (`wrapClears`, reader.tsx: once per gap the reader lays the blocks out with none cleared and measures them against the gap); a block above or below the card stays where it is. Clearing every such block pushed a video far above the card below it, so the card seemed to jump above the video. The tray card reads "In a floating card over the article" meanwhile.
- Every note, annotation, and edit jumps back to its exact position in the article: the jump button (the locate glyph) on a note's header opens the reader on the note's first source and flashes the quote (double-clicking a note card opens its editor instead — in the tray, on the notes full page, and on the floating card; a note shows no source chips under its body, only the quotes it holds, each pointing back to its source); on an annotation card it is the existing jump to the anchor; on an edit row it scrolls the open document to the edited block and flashes it, while the block is still in the document. **A quote points back on its own** (`lib/notes/quote-sources.ts`, `components/markdown.tsx`): a blockquote in a note whose words are a source's — the quote Add to notes wrote, or a quote dropped in — jumps to that source on a click, and a line under its words names the document (red, with "unresolved", when the source orphaned). No row of source chips renders under a note: the header's jump button reaches the note's first source, and every quote in the note reaches its own. **A quote deleted from its note takes its source with it** (`sourcesLeftByQuotes`, `lib/notes/quote-sources.ts`, on `PATCH /api/notes/[noteId]` with content): a source a quote covered before the edit and no quote covers after it is deleted, so the mark in the reader unpaints on the next refresh and no longer points at a note without the words; a source no quote ever covered (a paraphrase the assistant wrote) stays. In an open editor this happens when the editor closes, not with each auto-save (`keepSources` on the editor's saves, `pruneSourcesFrom` — the text the editor opened on — on its closing write): a quote deleted and brought back in the same sitting, by Ctrl+Z or Cancel, keeps its source. A deleted note takes every source with it (cascade), and the reader fades its marks at once (`dissect:note-removed`). **A quote dropped on a closed note is one write** (`appendQuote`, `components/outline/use-outline.ts`): `PATCH /api/notes/[noteId]` with `append` and `addSource` together, so a tab closed during the drop never keeps the words without their source; a note open in an editor takes the words into its draft and the source at once; a quote whose place the document no longer has still lands its words (`onSourceLost: "keep"`: the route appends without that source and answers `sourceDropped`), and the pill says why it has no source. An annotation dropped on a closed note is one write the same way: `append` with `copySourcesFrom`. A quote dropped into an open editor attaches its source at once; Cancel takes the words back out and gives up the sources the sitting attached (`removeSources`). The card blooms as the words land.
- **Quotes from the reader by drag** (`lib/quote-drag.ts`): a drag that starts on a highlighted passage carries the passage — its anchor, one segment per block, and its text — under the drag's own type, with a small card of the first words under the pointer in place of the browser's ghost; a **bookmark** at the left of every hovered block in the reader, transcript lines included (`BlockBookmark`, reader.tsx), drags the whole block the same way, the block its anchor. Let go in a note's editor, the quote lands at the text position under the pointer, on a line of its own, as blockquote lines: while the drag is over the text a caret drawn by the editor, which never blinks, stands where the quote will land (`note-editor.tsx`, `caretPositionFromPoint`). Let go on a note card outside its editor, the quote is added at the end of the note. Either way the passage's anchor becomes a source of the note (`PATCH /api/notes/[noteId]` `{addSource}`, resolved through the ladder like a source on a new note), so the quote points back; in a section's composer the source waits for the note the composer creates and attaches then. A quote let go on the tray off every note makes a new note with the quote and its source (`addDroppedNote`, `POST /api/notes {sectionId, content, source, segments, top}`) in the section under the pointer — at its top on the section's title row, else at its end; below every section, at the end of the last one — and the tray shows it; the section lights while the quote is over it. A highlight held in the reader's text does the same, and an annotation held there makes a new note holding its annotation reference. When a quote lands, the reader lets go of the selection it came from. A drop anywhere else ignores the quote, and a drop outside the app pastes its text. The drag reads the selection as it is when it starts (`captureSelection`), so it never waits on the toolbar. A **hold on a highlight** in the text — a mark with a source: a highlight, a comment's span, a note's quote, a tool's mark — lifts its passage (`watchHold` with `words`, `lib/card-drag.ts` kind `quote`). Words are for selecting first: a mouse stays on the mark for `WORDS_HOLD_MS` (500 ms), the mark rises with a shadow and the grab cursor (`mark[data-held]`), and only the move after that lifts the quote; the quote follows the pointer as a ghost of its first words, and let go on a note card of the tray or on the floating card it is added at the end of the note with the mark's anchor as its source, like a quote let go on a card outside its editor. A press that moves before the hold is a selection, as ever, and a press that ends where it began is the click that opens the annotation, however long. A touch never lifts a quote from the words: its long press selects the word, and the annotation is carried from its card's head row. The article stops selecting while the ghost is out.
- **Merged notes keep their seams** (`lib/notes/join.ts`): Join text puts every later note's text under a subtitle — the note's title, or "Merged note" when it had none — written as a level-six heading, which the note draws small and dimmed over a rule, in the rendered note and in the editor alike. The subtitle is a line like any other: delete it and the texts read as one.
- Notes have two views, per browser and per project (`unitos-notes-view:<notebookId>`; the tray and the notes full page share it). **Collapsed**, the default: every accepted note folds to one line — its title (its gist when it has none) and its source count (its tooltip: how many, then each document once, "4 sources · How Reading Shapes Memory"). The line is the one control that opens the note; on a pointer that hovers the row shows its id and its select circle on a hover or a focus (the circles stay on every row while a note is selected; on touch the circle stays and the id waits for the open note), and in the tray a row is 44px tall. An open note carries Copy, History, and Delete on its header row (on a hover where the pointer hovers, at rest on touch); a pending note carries Accept and Reject (✕) there (on a coarse pointer these small buttons, the id, and the select circle take a hit area about 12px taller than they draw, `touch-hit.ts`, and ✕ stands 4px further from Accept), and in the tray its body shows three lines until a click on it, or the keyboard queue landing on it, opens it whole. Over the pending queue — the tray's, and the notes full page's in its head, where it takes the place of a pending count and a key hint — Accept all accepts every pending note it shows; Enter and Backspace still accept and reject the one the keys are on, and e opens its editor; Accept all's tooltip names the keys, and the pencil's reads Edit. On a note's control, ↑ and ↓ move the focus to the same control on the note above or below; Alt+↑ and Alt+↓ move the note one place, and past its section's first or last place into the section above or below. Accept, Reject, and Delete hand the focus to the next note; a closed composer hands it to its section's + Note. A search keeps the view: a row stays a row and shows the note's first line that holds the match, started a little before it when it sits far into a long line, the match lit (`.search-hit`, `lib/search-hits.ts`); an open note lights the words in its title row and rendered body alike. The field takes every key at once and the list follows (`useDeferredValue`). The note being edited stays in the list, its editor open, until the editor closes (`editing-notes.ts`); a search that finds nothing says so ("No notes match"), and that line alone shows; clearing the search puts the view back. The gist is one short phrase, written by AI, that says what the note says and fits the row: at most 5 words and 30 characters (14 in Chinese), in the note's language, no trailing period (`lib/prompts/gist.ts`; `POST /api/notes/gist` writes it to `Note.gist` in one batched call when collapsed rows render without one; a content edit clears it). Until the gist arrives the row shows the note's first words, cut at a word boundary — never with an ellipsis. A row narrower than the gist or the title ends at the last whole word that fits (`WordLine`, `components/outline/word-line.tsx`; in Chinese, at a character). That fallback is the text the reader sees, never the markdown behind it: `lib/markdown-preview.ts` takes the markers off with the note's own parser (`parseNote`), so a stack of effects reads as plainly as one — a hand-rolled strip only knows the shapes it was written for, and left `**_word_**` reading as `_word_`. A chip reads as nothing and an image as its alt: a preview line has no room for a picture, and none for a URL. **Expanded**: every note shows whole — nothing clipped, nothing to scroll inside a card. One icon beside the search — Expand all / Collapse all, chevrons apart or together, its name in its tooltip — switches the view (the Annotations tab's is the same icon); a note's own chevron opens or folds that one note against the view until the view switches again. Pending notes never collapse: they are read before they are accepted. A jump to a note (an issue card) opens it if it is collapsed.
- Annotations have the same two views (`unitos-annotations-view:<notebookId>`): every card in the Annotations tab — highlight, comment, explanation, analysis, assistant conversation, simplified rewrite — carries the note structure (chevron, the highlight's color, the id) and collapses to its id and its gist by default (the AI-written phrase a note's row shows, its first words until it arrives); Expand all shows every annotation whole, the assistant conversations included, with no scroll inside a card. Clicking an annotation's mark in the text opens its card if it is collapsed. In the Annotations tab a press on a collapsed card's line opens the card and jumps to its mark too; an open card has no Jump or Delete of its own: they are in its ⋯ menu. A jump from the reader's own tab or menu takes the place of the page in the browser's history (`jumpToAnnotation`, annotation-card.tsx), so Back leaves the document instead of walking back through the jumps; from the annotations full page the reader is a new page. On the annotations full page every card names its kind, a highlight too. Every card's header ends in a three-dots menu (`annotation-menu.tsx`), in reach collapsed or not: **New note from this annotation** makes a note of it in a section the reader picks (`POST /api/notes {sectionId, fromAnnotationId}`: the annotation's text and copies of its anchors, accepted, at the end of the section; one section asks nothing); **Add to a note…** joins its text into a note the reader picks from a searchable list of the accepted notes, the most recently edited first (`POST /api/notes/merge`, join); Jump and Delete are there too, and Reopen on a resolved comment (§29), which has no Jump: it has no mark. The resolved comments list under Resolved, after Comments. Either way the annotation stays where it is, still painted in the article; a line under the header says what happened. An annotation on a figure, table, or equation carries a label — A1, A2, … counted over such annotations in document order, the same label the block shows in the article (`figureLabelBySource`, page.tsx) — and the label's tip says so; text annotations carry none, their highlight being their mark. Links keep their compact cards. Each group's label carries the symbol of the tool that made it — the comment bubble, Explain's question mark, Analyze's chart, the assistant's sparkle, Simplify's lines, the link chain — the glyph on the toolbar button and on the mark in the text, so a reader finds a comment or a link by the symbol they used. In the text the symbol is a small round chip that takes no room in the line: it stands right of the text column, level with the line the highlighted text ends on, in every view (`.mark-chip`; in the block reader `data-margin-chip`, laid side by side by `layMarginChips`, block-view.tsx), so a mark never pushes the words apart; clicking it opens the card. A highlight that holds a comment carries the comment's chip; a plain highlight carries none in the block reader, where a click on it opens its card, and a dot in its hue in the page editor. A highlighted figure, table, or equation shows the same symbol in its side label. Every kind of annotation carries one **kind color** everywhere (`--kind-*` in globals.css, `annotationKindColor` in `lib/annotations/kind.ts`): comment blue, explain red, simplify green, analyze teal, visualize magenta, the assistant violet, a link slate; a highlight carries its own hue. The color is the mark's fill and underline and its chip in the text (a comment's mark fills in it; a tool's mark underlines in it and fills on hover and while its card is open), the border of the card over the article, the border of the card in the Annotations tab and on the annotations full page, the border of the annotation beside a note on the notes full page, and the annotation reference row's icon and rule in a note (the row's href carries `&kind=`). The **annotations full page** (`/n/[notebookId]/annotations`, `components/panels/annotations-full-page.tsx`, the four-arrows button beside the tab's view toggle) is every annotation of the project, grouped by the document its first anchor is in — an article, a video, an audio document — in attach order, a detached document's after them, then Project for the sidebar assistant's conversations, anchored nowhere; within a document in reading order (the anchor's block, then its offset; a media document by time), a document's resolved comments last, under Resolved. The cards are the tab's (`components/panels/annotation-card.tsx`, shared): the kind named in the header with its symbol, the kind color on the border, the same three-dots menu and actions, Expand all and Collapse all shared with the tab; a document's title opens it in the reader. Links stay in the tab.
- Compare, on the notes full page: select two or more notes and press Compare in the selection bar. The compare view opens over the page with one pane per note — each its own scroller holding the whole note card, so the notes read and edit next to each other — Side by side (columns) or Stacked (rows); the layout choice persists per browser (`unitos-compare-layout`). Add note… adds a pane, ✕ removes one, and Esc or Notes closes the view.

**Other UX rules:**
- A note's jump button scrolls the reader to the note's first anchor and flashes the highlight. If the document isn't open, open it. A collapsed row keeps its source count; no chips render under a note.
- No pill, tab, select, or collapsed row cuts its text with an ellipsis. A label is written to fit (the Add-document tabs: PDF, image, or Markdown; Video or audio; Google Drive; URL; Library); a document title in the top bar's document pill, the document list, or a pane's document select is cut at a word boundary, the full title one hover away (`clipWords`, `lib/markdown-preview.ts`); a collapsed note or annotation shows its gist.
- **Folders.** A folder is a named group of a project's documents, and a folder can hold folders (`DocumentFolder`; `NotebookDocument.folderId`, null = the project itself). The document list opens on a press of the header's pill and closes on a second press; a pointer that stands still on the pill for 300ms opens it (the wait starts over while the pointer moves), so a pointer passing over on its way to + opens nothing. A list the hover opened closes when the pointer leaves it; a list the reader pressed open, or pressed inside, stays until a press outside, a second press, or Escape. ArrowDown on the pill opens the list; ArrowDown and ArrowUp move between its rows; Escape closes it and puts the focus back on the pill. On a narrow header (a phone) the project's title and then the pill narrow, so the + always shows beside the pill, and the list moves left to stay on the screen. Its Sort by row stays at the top as the list scrolls. The list draws the project's folders first, by title, then the documents outside every folder, then New folder. On a wide screen (md and up) hovering a folder's row opens the folder's own list beside it — its folders, then its documents, then New folder — one open list per level, so the tree reads as a menu; a press opens it too. Where the screen has no room beside the list, the folder's list opens under its row instead. On a narrow screen a press opens the folder's list under its row, and the open document's folders open on their own so the reader sees where they are. A folder's row shows how many documents it holds, its own folders counted in, and the folders on the open document's path are drawn bold. A folder's ⋯ holds New folder inside, Rename folder, Move to folder, and Delete folder; a document's ⋯ holds Move to folder, which lists the project itself and every folder; a folder never moves into itself. Delete folder asks first, under the row, in the app's own look, never the browser's; it moves what the folder holds up one level: nothing leaves the project. New folder draws the new row at once while the route runs. A dragged document or folder lands in its new place at once while the route runs, and goes back with the route's error under the list when the route refuses; while something in a folder is dragged, "Move to the project" draws over the Sort by row, so no row moves under the pointer. A folder is the reader's own grouping; no AI feature reads it. **Hold to drag** (`components/reader/tree-drag.ts`): an editor picks up a document or a folder by its row. A mouse lifts the row by moving 12px at once, or by holding still for 150 ms and then moving (the note card's numbers, `lib/hold-drag.ts`); a finger holds still for 300 ms (`TOUCH_HOLD_MS` from the same file, as for every hold to drag), since the list scrolls under the finger, and a finger that moves first scrolls. A press let go without a move is a click or a tap. The lifted row follows the pointer, tilted, and its place in the list dims. Over the upper or lower part of a row, a drop line — a clay line with a dot at its left end — says where the row lands in that list, in the project itself or in any folder's list; over the foot of a list (New folder) it lands last. The drop puts that list in the order the reader sees, with the row at the line, saves the list's whole order (`PUT /api/notebooks/[id]/order {parentId, items}`, `position` 0 first), and turns Sort by to Custom order. Over the middle of a folder's row, the row lights up and the drop moves the row into the folder; Move to the project, at the top of the list, takes it out of every folder. A row moved into another list — by a drag, Move to folder, or Delete folder — drops its old position and lists first there. Held over a folder, the drag opens its list; near a list's top or bottom edge the list scrolls; the list stays open until the drop; Escape cancels. A folder never lands in its own list or any list under it. Viewers never drag. Routes: `POST /api/notebooks/[id]/folders {title, parentId?}`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/notebooks/[id]/folders/[folderId]`, `PATCH /api/notebooks/[id]/documents/[documentId] {folderId}`, `PUT /api/notebooks/[id]/order`; the tree is `components/reader/document-folders.tsx`.
- **Sort by.** The top of the document list holds one pill, Sort by: Last edited (the default: folders and documents in one list, newest edit first), Custom order (the order a drag left each list in, with no categories: the rows a drag never placed first, newest edit first, so a document added since shows at the top, then the placed rows by `position`; `sortByPosition`), Added (the list as it was before sorts existed: folders by title, then documents in the order they were added; a list added over more than one week draws a category per week, oldest first, and the rows in it oldest first), Title, A to Z, Kind. Week added and Month added were two more sorts until 2026-10-07; a browser that kept either lists by Added. A document's last edit is the newest of its rich text's last save (`Document.richTextSavedAt`), the last change to a note or an annotation of the project written in it or quoting it (`Note.updatedAt`), and the day it was added; a folder's is the newest of the documents in it, its folders counted in, else the day it was made (`documentEditedAt`, `sortByEdited`). The pill opens the sorts under it, the current one checked. The sort orders every list — the project itself and each folder's own list — and Title and Kind put the list's rows in categories: a first letter or digit for Title (# for any other first mark, No title last), a kind for Kind. Typed letters in the open list go to the first row whose title starts with them, as a listbox does. A folder is a row like a document: its own title and the day it was made (`DocumentFolder.createdAt`) sort it, not what it holds, and under Kind folders are a kind of their own, Folder, listed first; the documents run PDF, Web page, Word file, Text file (a Markdown or text file, named as its import line names it), Slides, Sheets, Video or audio, Handwritten pages, Blank document, Generated document, Pasted text — read from the document (`kind`: the page's server read of the stored file's first bytes, `format`, `handwritten`, `generatedCommand`, the source URL, and whether it holds rich text). Added is `Document.createdAt`. Each category is a row — a chevron, its name, its count — that folds the rows under it. A sort moves nothing: folders open, drag, and take drops in every sort; a drop line reorders the list as the reader sees it in any sort, and turns the sort to Custom order; Alt+↑ or Alt+↓ on a focused row moves it one place, as a drag does. A drop or a move shows the notes' Undo pill (Moved, Undo, ✕, 12 s; Ctrl+Z presses Undo): Undo puts the lists it changed back in the Custom order they had, the row back in its list, and Sort by back to what it was. One choice per project in this browser (localStorage `unitos-documents-sort:<project>`, read in try/catch; a project with no choice of its own takes the browser's choice from before, `unitos-documents-sort`), so a drag that turns one list to Custom order leaves every other project's list alone; the folded categories are one choice per browser. The choice is not stored on the server. The order a drag leaves is: it is the project's, the same for every collaborator who picks Custom order. `lib/document-order.ts`, `components/reader/document-organize.tsx`. On a narrow screen the list moves left to stay inside the window.
- **Group by (notes).** The notes tray and the notes full page share one choice per browser (localStorage `unitos-notes-grouping`): Last edited (the default: every note in one list, newest edit first, by `Note.updatedAt`), Section, Document, Week added, Month added, Title, A to Z. `Note.updatedAt` moves only with a note's words or its status: a reorder, a move, a pin, a color, a merge or a delete that shifts the notes around it, and the gist keep every note's time (`lib/notes/order-writes.ts`). Section is where notes reorder and move between sections; every other grouping is a view that moves no note (no drop line draws), and in every grouping a hold merges a note into the one it covers and, in the tray, floats it out over the article. Every grouping but Section keeps the Note button and Command (its glyph alone, its name in its tooltip), which write in the first section, and every section's composer stays mounted, so a draft left open in any section reopens there and its note joins the list the moment the server has it (`components/outline/note-groups.tsx`). Every count of a section's or a group's notes counts its accepted notes.
- The text under the open selection popover keeps a selection tint (a mark of kind `selection`, the same color as the browser's selection): the browser's own selection goes the moment the assistant's command box or the comment box takes focus, but the tint stays until the popover closes. While the assistant runs a command on the passage, and after its suggestions land, the passage keeps only a thin mark in the assistant's color, never the fill: a long passage would otherwise be one block of color over the changes being reviewed. Every block of the passage (§5) keeps it, so the tint is the selection whole from the moment the pointer lifts, never the first block alone. The toolbox stands level with the tinted words' first line: a drag that starts on the title or the label over it reads from the first block, and the toolbox stands beside that block's words, not beside the title. The native selection over that mark paints nothing, so the two never stack. Reading mode only — edit mode's blocks are the browser's editable regions.
- A drag that starts on the article's words and lets go outside the pane — over the notes tray, the header — selects the article's words up to the point in the pane nearest where it let go, as if the drag had stopped at the pane's edge, and the toolbar opens on them. The tray and the header are never part of the selection.
- **Toolbar drafts** (`lib/toolbar-drafts.ts`): the words typed in the toolbar for a selection — the Add to notes comment, a Comment, a question in the assistant's box — are written to localStorage while the reader types (`unitos-toolbar-drafts:<documentId>`): each box keeps its own words (`components/kept-field.tsx`) and hands them over at most every 300 ms, at once on Enter, Escape, a blur, or the page closing, so a key renders the box alone, never the reader, keyed by the tool and the selection's block and offsets. Escape, a click in the article, a new selection, a reload, or a crash never throws them away: opening the same tool on the same words again (or on a selection over some of the same words of the block) shows them. A draft is cleared only when the server confirms the save — the note, the comment, the assistant's answer; a highlight takes a kept comment only while the comment box is open. Each write reads storage again and changes only its own entry, so two tabs on one document never erase each other's drafts; the storage event tells the other tab. The assistant's bar keeps its question the same way (the tool `assistant`, by the bar's words), until Send lands. **The panel's draft** (`unitos-assistant-panel-draft:<notebookId>`, `assistant-panel.tsx`): the words in the assistant panel's box, one draft per project, written at most every 300 ms and once when the page closes, and each message sent or queued until its answer is saved; a reload, a tab or document switch, New conversation, or another conversation keeps the box's words. A message whose answer fails, or a Stop before the answer's first words, takes the message out of the thread and puts it back into the box (its words, its quote, its attachments), with the reason under it; Send tries again. A Stop after the first words saves the question with the answer so far. **Card drafts** (`loadCardDrafts`, `writeCardDrafts`): the words typed in a card's box over the article — a comment card, a highlight's comment, a tool card's follow-up and the messages queued under it, the assistant chat's next question and each side chat's, a comment on an answer (`cardCommentKey`: the conversation and the quote) — are kept by the card's note id in memory and in localStorage, handed over by the box as the toolbar's are (`unitos-card-drafts`, the newest 50), so a card closed by Escape, ✕, a click, a reload, or a crash reopens from its mark with the words in its box; a draft goes once the box is sent and the server confirms it, or once the box holds the saved text again. Beside a comment's draft, the text it was typed on (`unitos-card-draft-bases`): a card that reopens after the comment changed elsewhere puts the draft together with the stored text, with marker lines, as a note's editor does, so a stale draft never writes over the change. Each tab writes only the drafts it changed over what storage holds now (`writeCardDrafts`) and reads the other tab's through the storage event (`onCardDraftsChange`). A comment card and a highlight's card save from the text they opened on (`baseContent`, §6 note edits): a comment changed elsewhere meanwhile keeps both versions, and the toast reads "Saved · both versions kept". A Comment from the toolbar whose save fails opens the toolbar again on its words, with the box, the words, and the reason under it; a dropped connection queues it as offline (§17). Escape in the Add to notes or the Comment box folds the box and leaves the toolbar: Add to notes gives the focus to its ▾; the Comment box and the Assistant box give it back to the selection (the page editor's text, the selection kept; the block reader's selected words), from where Tab goes to the toolbar's first row. A passage over 40 paragraphs (`MAX_SEGMENTS`, `lib/anchors/passage-limit.ts`) shows "Select at most 40 paragraphs to use the tools." in place of the tools. The link box (Ctrl+K in the page editor) closes the selection toolbar (`dissect:close-toolbar`).
- **Add to notes** (the toolbox's second row): one press makes a new note in the first section with the passage as its quote; an open tray shows it, and a folded tray or a phone's closed sheet stays closed while the Notes button blooms. Its ▾ half opens the panel in the tools' place — the tools fold away while it is open, and ▾ or Escape brings them back with the focus on ▾: a one-line field for the note's words ("Write the note (optional)"; Enter makes the note in the first section), the section rows under "New note in" when the project has two sections or more (with one, the press above is that row), and under "Which note?" the note picker (`components/reader/note-picker.tsx`: the accepted notes, the most recently edited first, each row two lines of the note's title or first words over its id and section, searchable; the arrows move through the rows and Enter picks the lit one — the first match until an arrow moves; Escape clears the search, then folds the panel; the list shows as many rows as the room allows and scrolls past them; the annotation menu's Add to a note uses the same picker). So the words go into a note in three presses: the words, ▾, the note. The page editor's right-click Add to notes adds in one press, as the toolbox's does. A new note is the passage as quote lines, a blank line, then the comment, with one source per segment (`POST /api/notes`). A picked note gets the same text at its end after a blank line and the passage as a new source (`PATCH /api/notes/[noteId] {append, addSource}`; `append` never rewrites the note's words), and the tray opens on it.
- Delete on a note, and Delete in the selection bar, ask nothing: the notes leave the list at once and the pill under the notes says "Note deleted" with Undo (`removeNotes`, `useOutline`). Nothing is deleted on the server while the pill shows: the DELETE runs when the pill goes — after twelve seconds, on its ✕, at the next delete or merge, or as the page closes (a keepalive request) — so Undo puts the notes back where they stood with no server call. A delete that fails puts the notes back and the pill says why. The pill is one for a merge, a delete, and a reject (`MergeUndoBar`, "Note rejected" with Undo), the newest change taking it; the workspace draws it on the page, so it shows with the tray open on any tab and with the tray folded. Reject, Undo reject, Undo merge, and a note's save change the list at once too, and a failed one puts the list back with the reason; Undo reject sends its request only after the reject's own has landed. A section's Delete — a bin at the end of the section's title field, which the rename pencil opens, so no bin rests beside a resting control — asks nothing either; the section leaves the screen at once and the server follows (a refresh before the server's delete never brings it back, `goneSections`): the server keeps the section whole in its History event first (`keepSection`, `lib/notes/removed.ts`: the section's row, every note kept as a removed note is, and the sections nested in it), then deletes it, and the pill says "“title” deleted · 3 notes" (a long title cut to 20 characters) with Undo, which puts the section back at once where it stood and runs History's Restore of that event (`undoSectionDelete`, `useOutline`, `backSections`). The header at rest keeps the title and its pencil on one row; a long title wraps beside the pencil. A rename shows the typed title at once; one that does not save keeps it: the field opens again on the typed title, marked Not saved, and Enter tries again (Escape puts the stored title back); a rename waiting in the offline queue shows the typed title, marked Not saved or Waiting to sync, until the queue lands it.
- A deleted note's marks answer at once, wherever the delete came from — a tool card's Delete, the on-mark card, the Annotations tab, the notes tray (`dissect:note-removed`): the mark fades (`.mark-out`), takes no clicks, and unpaints before the refresh; a failed delete, or Undo, puts it back (`dissect:note-restored`). A stored AI annotation's mark — Explain, Simplify, Analyze, Visualize, the assistant — is its underline and its symbol alone, no fill, while its card is closed (`.tool-mark`): the clay fill belongs to the live selection under the toolbar and to an open card's anchor, so a stored rewrite never reads as a selection; hovering fills it, and opening its card fills it. Marks that open something deepen on hover. An Explain, Simplify, or Assistant run keeps its mark after its card closes, until the server's copy lands.
- SIMPLIFY opens a translucent bubble to the right of the article, level with the selection, sliding in with a smooth animation. The selection stays tinted while the bubble is open. The document text never changes. The output persists as a note in the hidden Annotations section (like EXPLAIN), so it is still there when the reader leaves and comes back — listed under Simplified in the Annotations tab. Sentence mirroring: the prompt numbers the original sentences and the model appends a source marker ([[1]] or [[2,3]], at least one number) after each rewritten sentence. Every sentence in the bubble is lightly tinted; pressing one turns it solid and tints exactly its source sentences in the article. Both sides split sentences with the same function (src/lib/sentences.ts), so marker indices map back to exact offsets — never model-quoted text.
- Edit mode has no Edit button: double-click a text block to edit it in place. A fading hint card beside the article teaches this once: it is done after it plays to its end, or at the first double-click (`unitos-edit-hint`); it yields while a selection toolbar is open, which would cut its words. Where the article's right margin cannot hold the card (a tablet, a phone, a narrow window), the hint is a row under the pane instead: the pane gives up that height at its foot, so the hint covers no word and the lines in view stay where they are. Done, Esc, or a press anywhere outside the article, its format bar, the edit-mode controls, and the selection tools returns to reading — whatever was being typed saves first, and the format bar never outlives the mode. Selecting text in edit mode opens the same selection popover as reading mode; unsaved typing saves before any anchor is stored. In reading mode a keyboard selection opens it too: Ctrl/Cmd+A in the reader selects the article's blocks, not the page around them, and opens the toolbar on them; a selection Shift and the arrow keys extend opens it once Shift is let go.
- The edit toolbar: undo / redo, paragraph / h1 / h2 / h3 / bulleted list / numbered list, bold / italic / underline, text color, highlight, indent / outdent (two-space steps on the caret's line), remove paragraph. List markers live in the text ("- ", "N. "); a list made from an empty paragraph starts with its first marker and the caret after it. Typing "- ", "* " or "+ " at the start of a paragraph makes a bulleted list, "1. " a numbered list, "# ", "## ", "### " a heading; Enter in a list starts the next item with its marker, and Enter on an empty item takes the marker away. Text color: black (the default ink, `color-ink`), the four named hues, and a color wheel for any other color (`color:#rrggbb`). Highlight: the highlight button paints the selection in the last highlight color (yellow at first), and its color wheel picks another (`highlight:#rrggbb`). One color and one highlight per range; a new one replaces the old one on the same range (`lib/text-style.ts`). Text the reader typed or changed reads in the default ink like every other word; the History panel lists the changes.
- Undo and redo, on both editing surfaces, under the same two symbols — an arrow curving back and its mirror — and under Cmd+Z and Shift+Cmd+Z (Ctrl elsewhere). A note's history lives in its editable (`lib/note-editable.ts`), which already held it: the buttons are the same two steps. The article's edits are server calls, so its history is a stack of steps, each knowing how to take itself back and how to do itself again — a style is its own opposite, a format and a text edit remember what they replaced, an inserted paragraph is removed, and a removed one comes back through `/api/blocks/restore` with its own id, so anchors on it heal instead of orphaning. Undo settles the block being typed in first, so Cmd+Z after typing takes the typing back rather than the change before it. The article's history is the open document's, not edit mode's: it lasts until another document opens, so Cmd+Z still takes back the last change after Done — leaving the mode is not a reason to lose it. The keys are handled only when the article's history has a step to take: a text box or an editable the reader is typing in keeps its own undo, and with an empty stack the browser's own undo runs, untouched.
- Copy is the browser's on every surface: highlighted text anywhere copies with Ctrl/Cmd+C, and the toolbar carries no Copy button of its own. The article is the one place the browser needs help — the selection toolbar tints the highlighted text itself, and painting that tint replaces the block's marks, which takes the browser's selection with it — so while the toolbar is open, Ctrl/Cmd+C copies the anchor's `quotedText`, the same words a working native copy would have produced. A real native selection anywhere on the page wins, and so does any text box: there the browser copies as it always does.
- Cmd+B / Cmd+I / Cmd+U (Ctrl on Windows and Linux) style the selection on every surface that holds styled text, and a second press on the same words takes the style off: the reader's edit mode (`lib/markdown-style.ts` reads the keys, the same code path as the bar's B, I, U), the note editor everywhere it renders (`lib/note-editable.ts`, which owns the keys inside the editable — handling them again outside would toggle each press twice), and the comment boxes, whose text is markdown (the selection is wrapped in `**`, `*`, `<u>`). Markers hug the words: spaces at the selection's ends stay outside them, so the second press finds exactly what the first one styled. A reply is plain text, not markdown, and takes no styling.
- AI text cites document blocks as [block <id>] (the tags from the cached document prefix). Everywhere markdown renders — bubbles, chat, annotations, notes — the tag becomes a ¶ chip that scrolls the reader to that block and flashes it.
- The Assistant from the selection popover is conversational: the first command opens a miniature chat card beside the article; later turns send with the conversation history and the same anchor. The card resizes from its corner (native resize handle) up to 8px short of the pane's right edge, so its buttons stay in view. Plans still go through the approval card (or run in Auto), and the chat narrates the outcome. Every assistant chat can be stopped mid-turn: the Run/Send button becomes Stop while a turn is in flight — the request aborts server-side as well, and the stopped message goes back into the box. Closing the card with ✕ or Escape stops no run: the turn finishes and is stored, and the mark reopens it. A follow-up that fails, or is sent offline, goes back into the box with the reason under it (`data-send-error`), and no error turn is added; typing clears the reason. The question sent from the toolbar leaves the field for a line above it as it is sent, so the next question can be typed while the answer runs; a failed run says so in that box, under the question, and a stopped or failed run puts the question back in an empty field. An answer that lands while the reader has selected other words leaves their toolbar and selection alone: its card opens beside its words when they are in view, else a toast, Answer ready, carries Show, which opens the card and brings the words back. The plan the conversation proposes shows in its card, under the answer, and only while that card is open: it closes with the card and comes back when the card reopens from its mark. A tool conversation's plan (Explain+, Simplify+) shows under its turns in its own card the same way. The plan card at the window's foot is the side panel's alone. In a card the plan is its action rows and Accept N actions with Reject, under the answer that proposed it and scrolling with the turns, so a short card (a phone) shows the answer from its start and keeps the box its last row: no title, no second copy of the reply; the chat's line "Proposed 2 actions" shows only for a plan that came with no reply. A side chat shows its own plan and a side chat with no answer yet shows none; the conversation's plan waits in the conversation, and Back shows it again. When the first answer opens the card, the card's box takes the focus from the toolbar's box (and from the bar's), with any words typed there while the answer ran; Start side chat and Ask about this put the focus in the box too, so the next words typed land in a box, never on the page. A second card opens at its words, in view: an older card above it that reaches it gives up height and its body scrolls. On a phone a card taller than the room above the bottom bar gives up height the same way, so its box stays above the bar.
- Comment annotations show a small comment icon right after their span (SVG only — the block's DOM text stays the stored text). Clicking it opens the comment in a card with the same docking, dragging, and connector line as the other tool blocks; for an editor the card's Resolve resolves the comment (§29), and a resolved comment paints no mark and no icon. Every card a mark opens has one head: the kind, then ✓ (a comment: Resolve), Link across texts (a highlight: the next words the reader selects close the link), Delete (the trash), and ✕; a highlight's card holds the four colors and its comment under it. On a touch screen the head's buttons are 36px and the colors 28px, the toolbox's sizes, and a mark's chip is a 24px target.
- Stored EXPLAIN, ANALYZE, and SIMPLIFY annotations reopen their bubble: clicking the annotation's mark in the text (a figure's or table's side label) opens the bubble beside it with the saved content (sentence mirroring included for SIMPLIFY). Other annotation marks focus their card in the Annotations tab.
- Floating cards are freely moveable: drag the card header. Docking sets the initial position only.
