The vertical ruler at the canvas's left does the same for the top and bottom margins of the page that holds the caret. Both follow the zoom. Show ruler (View) turns them off, kept per browser. They show in Editing and Suggesting only — in Viewing nothing on them can be dragged — and neither shows on a pane narrower than 600 px (a phone), where the room above the first line is the reader's.
- **Indentation options** (Format > Align & indent; `toolbar/indent-dialog.tsx`): Left and Right in inches (centimeters in Chinese), Special indent (None, First line, Hanging) with its value, Cancel and Apply. The dialog shows the paragraph where the selection starts; Apply sets every selected paragraph, one undo step, with the attributes the ruler drags. Under a hanging indent, Left is where the first line starts and the other lines start the value further in (`indentLeft` is Left plus the value, `indentFirstLine` minus the value). Apply is off while the indents would leave less than half an inch of text.
- **Borders and shading** (Format > Paragraph styles; the Styles menu's last row and Search the menus; `toolbar/borders-dialog.tsx`, `toolbar/borders.ts`): Google Docs' dialog — Position (all, top, bottom, left, right, and between paragraphs when two or more are selected), the line's width, dash, and color, the paragraph padding, and the background color, with a preview — sets every paragraph the selection touches, one undo step, a suggestion in Suggesting mode; Reset takes every line and the background off. A side is `borderTop` … `borderLeft` and the line between `borderBetween` ("<width pt> <dash> #rrggbb <padding pt>"), the background `shading` ("#rrggbb <padding pt>"); the Word download keeps them (`w:pBdr` with `w:between`, `w:shd`), and an import of a Word file keeps its own (`parse/docx.ts`). Paragraphs with the same borders and shading, one after another, draw one box: the top line over the first, the bottom line under the last, the line between inside, one background with no gap (a bar alone keeps css/import.css's rule). In dark mode a black line draws in the table grid's sand, and the words on a light background keep the light theme's ink.
- **The contents panel** (`components/docs/page/outline.tsx`; code keeps `outline`), Google Docs' left panel, in a blank document the place of the Contents menu: the document's headings under the back arrow (a document has one tab, so the panel names none) — the Title and Heading 1–6, never the Subtitle — each nested under the heading above it. The heading that owns the top of the view is marked in clay as the page scrolls; a press scrolls to the heading and puts the caret there. The back arrow closes the panel; while it is closed, a round button at the canvas's top left opens it (Show the contents), 50 px from the canvas's left edge, or 32 px while the vertical ruler shows. When the margin beside the text column is thinner than the button (a phone, a tablet, a narrow split), the button is on the toolbar's row instead, where it covers no words; the canvas measures the room and the toolbar reads it (`outlineRoom`, `page/store.ts`). The button is always there: when the page moves left for the cards, it stays 16 px left of the page, down to the canvas's edge (4 px, 20 px beside the vertical ruler). It opens by itself on a new, empty document when the pane has the room. Its open state is kept per document, and its right edge drags its width (163–280 px). A pageless column fits in the room left beside the open panel, so no line goes under the notes tray. On an import the contents (§26) stand under the headings when the document has them, under a line and with no second Contents title: its parts, each a jump that scrolls to where the part starts and flashes it, the current one marked in clay; with none stored, the panel asks for them and offers Generate contents to an editor. When every part starts at one of the headings, the parts list nothing the headings do not, and the panel shows the headings alone.
- **The outline panel** (`components/docs/page/outline.tsx`), Google Docs' left panel, in a blank document the place of the Contents menu: the document's headings under the back arrow (a document has one tab, so the panel names none) — the Title and Heading 1–6, never the Subtitle — each nested under the heading above it. The heading that owns the top of the view is marked in clay as the page scrolls; a press scrolls to the heading and puts the caret there. The back arrow closes the panel; while it is closed, a round button at the canvas's top left opens it (Show the contents), 50 px from the canvas's left edge, or 32 px while the vertical ruler shows. When the margin beside the text column is thinner than the button (a phone, a tablet, a narrow split), the button is on the toolbar's row instead, where it covers no words; the canvas measures the room and the toolbar reads it (`outlineRoom`, `page/store.ts`). The button is always there: when the page moves left for the cards, it stays 16 px left of the page, down to the canvas's edge (4 px, 20 px beside the vertical ruler). It opens by itself on a new, empty document when the pane has the room. Its open state is kept per document, and its right edge drags its width (163–280 px). A pageless column fits in the room left beside the open panel, so no line goes under the notes tray. On an import the contents (§26) stand under the headings when the document has them: its parts, each a jump that scrolls to where the part starts and flashes it, the current one marked in clay; with none stored, the panel asks for them and offers Generate contents to an editor. When every part starts at one of the headings, the parts list nothing the headings do not, and the panel shows the headings alone.
- **Headers and footers** (`components/docs/page/header-footer.tsx`): the header starts 0.5 in below the page's top edge and the footer ends 0.5 in above its bottom; one taller than its margin pushes the text in. A double-click in a page's top or bottom margin, Header or Footer (Insert), or the chords (hold Ctrl+Alt, press O then H or F) edit it in place, with Google Docs' bar at its inner edge: the label, Different first page, and Options (Header format / Footer format: the margins and Different first page; Page numbers; Remove header / Remove footer). **Different odd & even** (the header bar and Header format): the even-numbered pages get a header and a footer of their own, labeled Even page header and Even page footer, and the other pages' read Odd page header and Odd page footer; a page is even by its number, as in Word, and Different first page still wins on the first page. Page numbers go on both, and the Word download keeps them. Escape or a press in the text leaves, and the text takes the keys again. While a header or footer is edited, the toolbar formats its text: Undo, Redo, the font, the size, Bold, Italic, Underline, Text color, and Align act on it, and so do Search the menus' Format commands. The controls a header cannot hold are off: Paint format, Styles, Text background, Insert link, Add comment, Insert image, Line & paragraph spacing, the lists, the indents, and Clear formatting. A header or footer saves as the text does (`editSetup`, `page/store.ts`): a pause of 700 ms, or 3 s of steady typing, sends the page setup (`PATCH {pageSetup}`); a failed save tries again with a growing wait of up to 10 s, as the text's; leaving the page with a change not saved tries one last save and asks the browser to warn. Page numbers (Insert): in the header or the footer, on the first page or not, starting at any number from 0 to 999; a right-aligned page number field draws each page's own number. Search the menus also has the four presets (header or footer, every page or from the second page) and Page count, which puts the page count at the caret of the header or footer being edited.
- **Print** (Ctrl+P, the toolbar's Print): the pages alone, one sheet each at the page's size (an `@page` rule the canvas writes), at 100%, without gaps, borders, rulers, or panels. Every spacer loses the gap between two pages, so each page prints the lines it shows, with its header, its footer, its footnotes, and the pinned header rows it repeats. Show print layout off prints the full pages all the same. The Unitos layer does not print: no marks, no chips. A pageless document prints as one flow over sheets of its page size, with its margins. A bookmark does not print; its ribbon keeps its room, so the lines break as on screen.
- **File > Download** (Search the menus: download, export, docx, pdf, markdown; `components/docs/page/download.ts`): Microsoft Word (.docx), PDF Document (.pdf), Plain Text (.txt), Web Page (.html), and Markdown (.md), each file named by the title. Microsoft Word is made on the server from the stored rich text, after the typing waiting to save (`GET /api/documents/[documentId]/export?format=docx`, viewer; `richTextDocx`, `lib/docs/export.ts`): the named styles are Word's Normal, Title, Subtitle, and Heading 1–6; a run keeps its marks (a highlight is shading, small caps Word's small caps); a paragraph keeps its alignment, spacing, indents, borders, tab stops, and flags; a list is Word numbering in the levels the page draws (its preset's or its own: a glyph format is Word's level text, a legal level's numbers Word's legal numbering, a level that draws no marker Word's number format none with no tab or space after it), a nested list one level down, each level at the depth an import's page sets (`listIndents`), with a tab stop at the words' place where the marker does not hang, else a half inch a level with a hanging indent wide enough for its widest marker, a checklist line ☐ or ☑ and struck through when ticked; a table keeps its column widths, merged cells, backgrounds, borders, padding (an import's `cellPadding`), and pinned header rows; an image keeps its size, crop, rotation, and alt text (an uploaded one read from the store, a linked one fetched, one Word cannot read redrawn as a PNG); a heading is a bookmark, so links to headings and bookmarks and a table of contents' entries jump; footnotes are Word footnotes; a suggestion is a Word tracked change — added words an insertion, removed words a deletion (another person's added words struck are a deletion), every run of a block added or removed whole — with its author's account name, Assistant for the assistant's (in the reader's language), else "Unitos", and the time its id holds; a comment is a Word comment on its words, with its author's account name (else "Unitos") and its time, and a resolved comment stays out (`docxComments`: the comments of the projects the reader can open; words in a footnote or a code block carry none); the page setup is the section: paper size, orientation, margins, the header and the footer with their page number and page count, Different first page, the first page's number, and the page color. A chip or an equation goes in as its words. A run-in heading is a heading paragraph with its mark hidden (Word's style separator), followed by a space, so Word runs it into the next paragraph. Markdown and plain text write it as a heading line. The web page, Markdown, and plain text are the document as Viewing mode shows it, without its suggestions (`withoutSuggestions`, `lib/docs/blocks.ts`: added words go, removed words stay, a format change goes back). The web page is the page's HTML with every named style drawn whole (`namedStyleSheet`), its lists drawn as the page draws them (`listSheet`), its images inside, its equations as MathML, and its tables of contents drawn; Markdown is Copy as Markdown's (spaces outside the emphasis marks, a numbered line's marker as the page draws it ("(a)", "[12]"), a bullet and a line of a list with no marker as "-" (Markdown has no list without markers), an equation as `$…$` or `$$…$$`, a chip as its label, a footnote's number as `[^n]` and each footnote as a `[^n]:` line at the end), with the images at the end; plain text is one line per paragraph of the paragraph index (a list line after its marker as the page draws it, "(a) " or "☑ ", a line of a list with no marker as its words alone; an inline equation as `$…$`), a footnote's number as `[n]`, and each footnote at the end after its number. PDF Document opens the print dialog with the toast "Choose Save as PDF". An import's figure object (§30) goes into each file as the page draws it, its caption under it: in Word a PDF figure is its crop at the size the PDF prints it, rendered on the server from the stored PDF (one page at a time, within 40 s; past that the caption alone), its caption in the marks the page sets it in, and a web figure is its images, or its chart drawn as a PNG; the web page carries the images inside; Markdown puts the crops and the app's own images at the end as data and links the images of other sites; plain text has the caption's line; a video or an embed is its caption. A page start adds nothing to any file.
- **File > Make a copy** (`components/docs/page/make-copy.tsx`): Google Docs' Copy document dialog: Name ("Copy of <title>", selected), Copy suggestions (off; comments are annotations, which stay with the original), Cancel, OK; on in every mode for an editor of the project, also on a shared import (§30), which the copy makes the reader's own to edit; off for a viewer. `POST /api/documents/blank {notebookId, title, copyOf, suggestions}` (editor of the project, viewer of the original) copies the rich text and the page setup: every paragraph gets a fresh block id, a link to one of its headings follows the heading, and the Block rows are derived from it. With the box off, the copy reads as if every suggestion were rejected; on, the suggestions stay with their authors. The notes, annotations, comments, and history stay with the original. The copy sits in the original's folder and opens in this tab. A copy of an import (§30) is a blank document (`importRev` null, no file hash, no address), so it opens in Editing; it keeps the media of its figures (new `FigureMedia` rows for the media its text names), the captured images those media name, the PDF when a figure is a crop (it counts toward storage), its page labels, and its references. Re-parse answers 400 for a document with no file and no address.
- **File > Details** (Search the menus, any mode; `page/details-dialog.tsx`): Document details, Google Docs' dialog: Location (the project, then the folders the document sits in), Owner (the project's owner), Modified (when the text was last saved), Modified by, and Created (`GET /api/documents/[documentId]/details?notebookId=`).
- **Tools > Compare documents** (Search the menus; `page/compare-dialog.tsx`, `page/compare.ts`): Google Docs' comparison, made the way Make a copy makes its copy. The dialog lists the project's other documents; Compare copies this document as "Comparison of <this> and <other>", puts the other document's differences in the copy as suggestions by the reader, and opens it — this document stays as it is. The blocks line up by their words; between the blocks they share, a block of the same kind with words alike pairs up and only its changed words are suggested, never cutting a word, and what is left is suggested out with the other's blocks in their place as one suggestion; lists, quotes, and tables (of the same rows and columns) line up inside the same way. Each difference is its own suggestion, so Accept all gives the other document's words and Reject all this one's. The same words make nothing, and a document without rich text (a block document) has no text to compare.
- **Tools > Translate document** (Search the menus, a project editor; `page/translate-dialog.tsx`, `page/translate.ts`): a translated copy, as Make a copy makes one, without its suggestions, named "Translated copy of <title>", in English or Chinese (Simplified), translated by §19's route (`POST /api/documents/[documentId]/translate`, DeepL). Each paragraph, heading, list line, table cell, and footnote takes its translation in place, with the marks all its words shared, and a figure's caption takes its translation without its marks; code, and a paragraph that holds an equation or another object, keep their words, and a toast says how many. A failed translation leaves no copy.
- **Tools > Line numbers** (Search the menus, pages format; `page/line-numbers.tsx`, `ext/line-numbers.ts`): Show line numbers (`PageSetup.lineNumbers`) numbers each line of the text in the left margin, from 1 on each page (Restart each page) or on through the document (Continuous); the lines of paragraphs, headings, list items, code, and an empty paragraph count, while a table, a table of contents, a figure, and the footnotes take none. Suppress line numbers in the selected paragraphs takes their lines out (a paragraph's `suppressLineNumbers`). Print keeps the numbers; pageless shows none; the Word download numbers the lines the same way (`w:lnNumType`, `w:suppressLineNumbers`).
- **The title row**: the title in Caprasimo 18 px, as every title in Unitos; a 1 px border under the pointer (Rename), a 2 px clay one while renaming. A click selects the whole title. Enter keeps it and returns to the text, Escape puts the old title back, and leaving the field keeps it. An empty title is "Untitled document", drawn gray. An untitled document takes its first line's words as its title (trimmed, at most 200 characters) at the first save once a line follows the first line; a title the reader chose never changes. The status stands at the toolbar row's right end, not here, for the text and the page setup (a header, a footer, the margins) alike, and it carries the app's failed writes too (notes, annotations), so the app's own line in the top bar hides while it shows: a write that did not land shows in one place, and an app write in flight does not spin this cloud over a document that is saved: the arrows while a change waits or saves, then the cloud with a check; the words come only when a save is in trouble ("Trying to connect…" when the request did not reach the server, "Not saved" when the server refused or failed it, or when the document is saved and a note's or an annotation's save failed; the popup says which); a failed save keeps its words through every retry and the typing after it until a save lands — the document's save retries with a growing wait, at once when the browser is back online or the tab is shown again, and a note's Not saved stays until a write to that note lands (its retry, §6), however many other writes land meanwhile (`readUnconfirmed`, lib/save-state.ts) — on the toolbar's row a caption that came and went with every save would move the controls beside it, and the symbol and the popup say the rest. A press on the status opens Google Docs' status popup: a clay-100 header with the state (red when a save failed) and one line on what it means. A title too long for the row is cut with an ellipsis. The row is hidden on a pane narrower than 600 px (a phone): the title stays in the document pill of the app's top bar, File > Rename brings the row back, and the status and the version clock are on the toolbar's row.
- **The header** (the title row, the toolbar, and the ruler) shows while the reader is in the document: the last press or focus was in the page, the header, the card column, or one of the page editor's menus and dialogs. A press or the focus in the other pane, or in this pane outside the page (the Extract page), fades it out, over 200 ms after a 300 ms pause; the notes tray and the app's top bar work on the open document, and a press there leaves the header as it is. Pointing at it shows it; a click in the page, or the focus in the page from the keyboard, brings it back. It keeps its place, so the page never moves, and the text scrolled under it shows through. A document opens with it shown. It stays above the pages and the card column (the cards scroll under it) and under the Extract page and the conversation view, which cover it, the menus of the app's top bar (the document list, Share, History), and every dialog and menu (`css/layer.css`; `data-away`, docs-editor.tsx).
- **The notes tray.** Until the reader opens or folds the notes tray (§6), any document opens with it folded in a window narrower than 1000 px (a tablet held upright: beside the tray the article would be a phone's column), and a blank document in a window narrower than 1860 px, the whole toolbar beside the open tray at its default width; the rail stays, and the tray is one press away. Normal view only; an imported document opens as before. A press on a note's mark, a jump to a note or an annotation, Show all comments, and Add to notes (the tray opens on the new note) open the tray.
- **Side by side.** With two page editors in split panes, each one's keys, commands, menus, dialogs, and toasts act in its own pane: they are events on its text (`fireDocs`, `components/docs/typing/events.ts`), and the pane takes Add comment, the Unitos tools, and the toasts that come up from its own page editor. Hide the title row (Ctrl+Shift+F) answers only while the page editor has the focus or nothing does.
- **The caret**: Google Docs' 2 px bar, as tall as the text, 500 ms on and 500 ms off, solid after every move. Where a line wraps, one position ends a line and starts the next; there the browser's own caret shows. The canvas scrolls to keep the caret 8 px below the title row, the toolbar, and the ruler. While a drag selects — from the words, a margin, or the canvas — one owner scrolls the pane (`components/docs/page/margin-select.ts`), not the browser: the band is 24 px inside the pane's bottom edge and under the toolbar at its top; the scroll starts once the pointer is in the band's outer 6 px or past the edge, or rests in the band for 0.45 s, and grows with the depth. A drag along the last line in view selects that line. The selection is Unitos's, clay at 30% (the selection an article shows), while the page has focus, with the Unitos toolbar open or not. While the page has no focus or a menu is open over it (the right-click menu, a toolbar menu, a dialog, the link box, the comment field, Find, the title field), the selection stays drawn in a warm gray: sand-500 at 36%.
- **The toolbar** (`components/docs/toolbar.tsx`, `components/docs/toolbar/`), Google Docs' own, left to right:
  - **Search the menus** (Alt+/): finds every registered command (`components/docs/commands.ts`) and toolbar action by its Google Docs label or by Docs' own search words ("hanging indent", "all caps", "bullets & numbering"), with its menu and shortcut, and values such as "font size 14" or "zoom 150". With nothing typed it lists the six menus (File, Edit, View, Insert, Format, Tools), each with a ›: a press or Right lists that menu's commands, the rows search returns; Left or Escape goes back to the menus, and typing searches again. So a command whose name the reader does not know is still read, menu by menu, as Google's menu bar is read. A 100 px field above 1600 px of window, a symbol button below. It is how Unitos reaches what Google keeps in its menu bar, which Unitos does not have: File > Rename puts the focus in the title (the title row shows first when the menus are hidden); Edit > Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste without formatting, Select all, and Delete are the right-click menu's; View > Show non-printing characters; Format > Text > Strikethrough, Superscript, Subscript, Small caps, and Capitalization; Format > Paragraph styles and Align & indent open the toolbar's menus.
  - **Undo, Redo.** Print (Ctrl+P) and Show spelling suggestions (Unitos's red squiggles on or off; Ctrl+Alt+X) are not on the row: both are in Search the menus (File, Tools), the switch is in Preferences too, and the Viewing row keeps Print.
  - **Paint format**: a press copies the text and paragraph formatting where the selection starts (not links, not the list), and the next selection takes it. A second press within 500 ms keeps it on until Escape.
  - **Zoom**: Fit, 50–200%. A typed number clamps to 50–200. A press on the box opens the list; Escape closes it and gives the page the focus back.
  - **Styles**: Normal text, Title, Subtitle, Heading 1–3, and Heading 4–6 once the level above is in use. Each style has Apply 'X' and Update 'X' to match. Options: Save as my default styles, Use my default styles, Reset styles.
  - **Font**: More fonts, the five recent faces, then every face A–Z, each drawn in itself. A face with more than two weights opens its weights. More fonts adds Google Fonts families to My fonts.
  - **The size**: −, the box with Docs' list 8–96, +. − and + move every run one point, clamped to 1–400. A typed size floors to half a point. Mixed sizes leave the box blank. A press on the box opens the list; Escape closes it and gives the page the focus back.
  - **Bold, Italic, Underline, Text color, Text background** (Google's Highlight color, named for what it is: it is no highlight, it never lists in the Annotations tab): Docs' 8 × 10 palette with its color names, Text background's None, and the reader's custom colors with the add button and the eyedropper.
  - **Insert link, Add comment, Insert image** (Upload from computer, By URL).
  - **Align.**
  - **Line & paragraph spacing**: Single, 1.15, 1.5, Double, Custom: N. Add 10 pt or remove the space before and after. Custom spacing opens its dialog (from Search the menus too). On pages: Keep with next, Keep lines together, Prevent single lines, Add page break before.
  - **Checklist, Bulleted list, Numbered list**: split buttons. The left half toggles the list (Ctrl+Shift+7/8/9 too): a line outside a list goes into one where it stands, keeps its place, and joins the lists of the kind before and after it (`ListToggles`, typing/keys.ts); the right half opens Docs' presets.
  - **Decrease indent, Increase indent.**
  - **Clear formatting**: the text loses every mark but its links; the paragraphs lose alignment, spacing, indents, borders, and flags; the named style and the list stay. At a caret the next words get no style, and an empty paragraph loses its format and its mark's style too.
  - **In a table** (after Clear formatting, while the caret is in a table): Background color, Border color, Border width, and Border dash — the borders the cell's ▾ chose, else every border of the selected cells — and Table options, which opens its panel.
  - **At the right end**: the document's status and the version clock (the title row's, moved here so they show while the title row is hidden); Collapse (§28); the mode (Editing, Suggesting, Viewing; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Z is Editing, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+X is Suggesting, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+C or D is Viewing; a switch to Editing or Suggesting gives the page the keys at its caret, the selection kept). Hide the title row is a command, not a button: Ctrl+Shift+F and Search the menus > View (it also answers to "hide the menus"). The page editor starts with the title row hidden in a pane narrower than 600 px (a phone), shorter than 500 px (a phone held sideways), or of a split view (its pane header names the document; the reader tells the page editor it is in a split, so the title row is hidden from the first frame, the loading frame's too); the title stays in the document pill, the pane header, and File > Rename.
  - In Viewing mode, and for a viewer, the left side is Search the menus, Print, Add comment, and Zoom. Search the menus lists the Insert and Format commands off there. On a shared import (§30) it lists Editing mode, Suggesting mode, and Rename off, each with the reason under it.
  - Each tooltip names its shortcut the way the platform prints it (Ctrl+B, ⌘B) and shows 4 px under the control after 300 ms.
  - When the row is too narrow, a Unitos tool keeps only its symbol, then the mode's name folds, then whole groups go into More tools (⋯; the reader's bar keeps More), the least used first, not the rightmost: Zoom, Paint format and voice typing go first, then the font, the size, the styles, the colors, the indents and Clear formatting, Undo and Redo, Align and the spacing, the link and Add comment and Insert image, the lists, and Bold, Italic and Underline last; Search the menus stays on the row. A group the fold took that fits in the room left comes back, the most used first (on a phone Undo and Redo stay on the row). On a touch screen, whose keyboard has no Ctrl+Z, Undo and Redo fold last, after Bold, Italic and Underline. Viewing's row folds the same way: Zoom first, then Print, then Add comment; Search the menus stays. So Bold and a bulleted list stay one press away while the row is short. Search the menus is a white field when the toolbar is wider than 1568 px (Docs' at a 1600 px window), else its symbol.
  - The row is one Tab stop: Left and Right move, Down opens a menu, Escape goes back to the page. Every control of the row shows the focus the same way: a 2 px clay ring outside it (outside a New glow too). No toolbar button takes the page's focus.
- **Named styles.** A document's changes to a named style are stored on the rich text's doc node, one JSON attribute per style (`namedStyleNormal` … `namedStyleH6`, checked with Zod), and drawn by a style sheet the editor writes for itself (`namedStyleSheet` in `toolbar/styles.ts`, `ext/toolbar.ts`). An unchanged style stores nothing. Save as my default styles keeps the changes in the browser. An import stores the named styles its page's look sets the same way (§30).
- **List presets.** A list has nine levels, as the Google Docs API has them: each draws a bullet, or a counter (decimal, zero decimal, lower or upper alpha, lower or upper roman) in its glyph format, the words around the numbers ("%0.", "(%1)", "[%0]", "%0.%1."; `ListLevel`, `lib/docs/schema.ts`). A bullet may be empty: its level draws no marker, as Google Docs' glyph type NONE (a list with no marker: an import's bibliography, an algorithm's lines; no palette offers it). A preset is nine levels. The outermost list keeps its preset in `listStyle` (`data-list-style`), named as the Google Docs API names it (`BULLET_DIAMONDX_ARROW3D_SQUARE`, `NUMBERED_DECIMAL_NESTED`, `CHECKLIST_NO_STRIKETHROUGH`, …); null is the type's default. An outermost list whose levels are no preset's (an import's "a).", "[12]", or list with no marker) keeps the levels themselves in `listLevels` (`data-list-levels`: nine levels as JSON, checked by `listLevelsOf`), and a preset picked from a palette takes them off. The list's inline style sets each level's bullet or marker, and one sheet for every page editor draws them (`listSheet`: a bullet as the list-style-type, none for an empty one, a number as the ::marker's content); a nested list draws its level of the outermost list's. An import's outermost list also keeps where its page sets each depth (`listIndents`, `data-list-indents`, checked by `listIndentsOf`: one [left, first] in points a depth, up to nine, and for a marker that does not hang a third number, hang, where its words start from its start; `indentStyle`). Such a list draws no ::marker: each depth's words stand at left and its first line at left + first, and each line's marker is a box at the first line's start that reaches to where the page starts the words (−first where the marker hangs, else hang) or, with no such place, to the next half-inch stop, as Docs and Word set a tab after a marker; a marker wider than its box moves the first line's words on, so no marker stands left of its line's start. A depth that draws no marker has no box. The depths past the list's own go on a half inch a depth, set as the last one; a list without them draws its ::marker, a half inch a depth, and a checklist keeps its own inset. `toolbar/lists.ts` holds the nine levels of every preset, the palettes' and the ones a typed prefix gives (`BULLET_DASH`, `NUMBERED_ALPHA_ROMAN_DECIMAL`, …), and `listMarker` gives the marker a numbered line draws.
- **Restart and continue numbering** (`toolbar/lists.ts`). On a line of a numbered list the right-click menu has Restart numbering and Continue previous numbering. Restart numbering splits the list before the line, and the line's new list starts at 1; it does not show on a list's first line when the list starts at 1. Continue previous numbering numbers the list on from the list before it of the same kind and format (its preset and its own levels), and joins the two when they touch; it shows only when the numbering does not already go on. In Search the menus, Restart numbering is List options': the Numbering dialog asks "Restart numbering at" (1–9999). Each change is one undo step.

- **Keys** (`DocsKeymap`, `components/docs/extensions.ts`, and `ext/typing.ts`), Google Docs' document shortcuts: Ctrl+Alt+0 Normal text, Ctrl+Alt+1–6 the headings (a heading stays a heading), Ctrl+Shift+7/8/9 numbered list, bulleted list, checklist, Ctrl+Shift+L/E/R/J the alignments, Ctrl+[ and Ctrl+] the indent (a list line nests or lifts), Ctrl+\ (and Ctrl+Space) clears formatting, Ctrl+. and Ctrl+, superscript and subscript, Alt+Shift+5 (⌘⇧X) strikethrough, Ctrl+Shift+. and Ctrl+Shift+, move every run one point, Ctrl+Enter a page break, Ctrl+K a link, Ctrl+Alt+M a comment, Ctrl+Shift+C the word count; B, I, U, Z, Y as everywhere. Undo groups typing by the second; a selection change (an arrow key, a click) ends a step, and each image change is its own step (a slider's drag is one).
- **A page break** (Ctrl+Enter) splits the line at the caret and the second half, empty or not, starts the new page with the caret in it; at a line's start the whole line moves to the new page.
- **Typing** (`components/docs/ext/typing.ts`, `components/docs/typing/`), Google Docs' keys:
  - **Enter** splits the paragraph and keeps its style and the pending style. At the end of a heading, Title, or Subtitle it gives Normal text. On an empty list line it removes the bullet, or lifts the line one level.
  - **Backspace** at a line's start, in order: a list line loses its bullet and keeps its place: it leaves the list at the level's indent, the list splits there, and a numbered list's second part numbers on; a centered line goes left, a right-aligned one to the center; an indent goes; after a table nothing; otherwise the lines join and the lower line's style stays, unless the upper line is a list line. **Delete** joins the same way. In Suggesting mode a join is suggested as a removed break alone; a list line keeps its bullet and joins the line above, and a list's first line stays.
  - **Word keys:** Ctrl+Backspace, Ctrl+Delete, Ctrl+←/→, and double-click use Docs' words (don't, well-known); a drag after a double-click grows by whole words. Ctrl+↑/↓ goes to paragraph starts.
  - **Tab**, in order: the next cell, every selected paragraph, one list level, a first-line indent, else a tab. Shift+Tab never types a tab: anywhere in a list line it lifts the line one level, anywhere in a paragraph it takes back the paragraph's indent, and with nothing to take back it does nothing.
  - **Ctrl+Shift+↑/↓** moves paragraphs; list lines keep their numbering.
- **Autocorrect** runs on the word or line just ended. It never runs in code, on paste, or during IME input, and each change is its own undo step. The rules: a list from 17 prefixes (a list's next label as the list draws it continues the list: "4." after three lines, "15" after a list drawn "14"), a capital at a sentence's start, links (an email gets mailto:), smart quotes, Docs' 27 substitutions (-- then - gives —), and everyday spelling fixes with an Undo bubble; an undone fix never comes back. With Enable Markdown on (Preferences, off by default): # headings, * _ ** ~~ ` emphasis, [text](url), and ``` code blocks. Preferences are kept in the browser (a switch stored before it existed reads as its default); Show spelling suggestions and Show grammar suggestions (both on by default) are the squiggles' switches; Insert emojis using the colon character (on by default) lets ":" and letters open the "@" menu with emoji. The editor runs no Tiptap input rule and only Link's paste rule: Docs' own rules are the only ones.
- **The paragraph mark.** A style pressed at a paragraph's end, or set on a selection that reaches the end, styles the paragraph mark (`markStyle`). An empty line types in it, and a list line's bullet or number is drawn in it.
- **Paste** keeps the formatting it can map and never turns Markdown into formatting; an address becomes a link. Plain text becomes one paragraph per line in the style at the caret. Ctrl+Shift+V pastes plain text. An image pasted or dropped is uploaded (Images). Pasted HTML keeps only what a save keeps (`pastedHtml`, `typing/paste.ts`): a color becomes #rrggbb (a see-through one drawn over white; Word's highlight too), a size whole or half points (px × 0.75; em, rem, and % against 11 pt; 1 to 400), and what cannot be read goes, so the page never shows what a save drops. A table cell keeps its background. Word's list paragraphs (`mso-list:lN levelM`) become list items at level M, without the marker Word writes before each: a marker in Symbol or Wingdings, "o", or one character that is not a letter or a digit means bullets, any other numbers. A Google Docs list keeps each level's counter or bullet (the clipboard's list-style-type at each level), as its preset or its own levels; the clipboard holds no words around a number, so "(a)" pastes as "a.". A Google Docs checklist line becomes a checklist line, ticked or not, without its checkbox image; a ticked line's strikethrough is the checklist's, not the words'. Its images are copied into Unitos: a data: image shows at once and uploads, a remote one uploads when the browser may read it and else keeps its address, and each image takes the stored address when its upload ends, not as an undo step of its own; a data: image the server refuses goes, with the reason, and an image no save keeps (file:, cid:) goes. A file dropped on the page editor never becomes a project document (`components/reader/use-page-file-drop.ts`, `typing/drop.ts`): an image file, or an image dragged from another page, dropped anywhere on the page — on the words, in a margin, under the last line, or on the gray around the page — goes into the text where a caret at the drop point stands, and the drop line says where while the drag is over the page; a file that is not an image stays out, with the reason; the title row, the toolbar, and the rulers refuse files. Viewing mode changes nothing: an image dropped or pasted there says the page is in Viewing mode, with Switch to Editing. With Markdown on, the right-click menu has Paste from Markdown and Copy as Markdown. A project document's address pasted on its own shows "Tab to replace" under it (`typing/url-chip.ts`): Tab puts the document's chip in its place, its own undo step; moving the caret or typing leaves the link.
- **Capitalization** (Format > Text; `setCase`, `typing/format.ts`): lowercase, UPPERCASE, and Title Case rewrite the selected letters. Each run keeps its marks, each changed letter is its own step so the Unitos marks keep their ends, and the change is one undo step. Title Case gives each word a capital first letter and small letters after it, by Docs' words (don't, well-known); a word the selection starts inside goes on.
- **Find** (Ctrl+F): a bar at the top right showing "N of M"; every result in Unitos's search gold at 45%, the current one full gold; Esc, from anywhere in the bar, closes it and leaves the result selected. **Find and replace** (Ctrl+H, ⌘⇧H), a modal dialog: Tab stays in it, Escape closes it wherever the focus is (a click in the text too), and Ctrl+H on an open one puts the focus in Find. Match case, regular expressions, Ignore diacritics; Replace all is one undo step and says "Replaced N instances of “…”" ("Replaced 1 instance" for one); in Suggesting mode each match is a suggestion of its own. A straight quote finds curly ones, and ... finds ….
- **Also:** Keyboard shortcuts (Ctrl+/), voice typing (Ctrl+Shift+S, Tools, or the microphone in the toolbar: one press starts listening and opens the box at the left of the page as its listening card, with the language menu and the microphone that stops; the toolbar's microphone or the key again closes it, as every other surface's microphone works; the browser's speech recognition), non-printing characters (Ctrl+Shift+P), and Tools > Spelling and grammar: Show spelling suggestions (Ctrl+Alt+X or F7: the red squiggles on or off) and Show grammar suggestions (the blue squiggles on or off), each with a toast, kept in the browser, and in Preferences too.
- **Voice typing on every surface.** Every box where the reader writes a note or talks to an AI has a microphone button beside it (`components/voice/voice-typing-button.tsx`): the note editor's bar (the composer, a note being edited, the floating note), a reply, the note assistant, the sidebar assistant and its side chats, a comment on an answer, the reader's selection assistant and the assistant bar, the reader's assistant chat and its side chats, the conversation after Explain, Simplify, Analyze, and Visualize, Comment, a comment card and an annotation card, a link card, the page editor's comment card, Extract and Extract across the project, circle and ask on a PDF page, Stitch, the video assistant, a question about a video range, and a comment on a video moment. A click starts listening and a second click stops it; listening goes on through pauses. What is heard goes in at the box's caret as typing does (the box's own input event), so the box keeps its draft, its undo, and its save; nothing is sent until the reader sends it. "period", "comma", "question mark", "new line", and "new paragraph" type what they name, in English; a one-line box takes a space for a new line. While it listens, a card over the button shows the words heard so far and the language; it takes no clicks. A box above its button (the toolbar's assistant box) keeps its words in view: the card sits over the box. The words still being heard are typed at the caret before the box can close or send: a press outside the box and the button (Done, ✕, a click away), Escape, or Enter in the box types them first, and the service starts afresh so they never come twice; Enter then sends the whole box, or types its new line where Enter does not send. The language is one choice per browser for every surface (`unitos-docs-voice-lang`), picked in the page editor's listening box, else the app's language. The page editor's box and the buttons share one module (`lib/voice-typing.ts`, `components/voice/use-speech.ts`). The browser's speech service hears the words; Unitos stores no audio. Voice command (the notes tray's Command) is a different tool: it records audio and writes pending notes.
- **Spelling suggestions** (`typing/spelling.ts`, `typing/proofing.ts`, `components/proofing/`). Unitos draws its own red squiggle under each misspelled English word of the whole document, in Editing and Suggesting (none in Viewing), not only near the caret: decorations, worked out off the typing path — nspell with the English Hunspell dictionary (`public/spelling`) in a worker (`proofing/spell.worker.ts`), the paragraphs on screen first, 200 at a time while the page is idle, and again only for a paragraph that changed, 250 ms after the change. The word the caret is ending is not marked until the caret leaves it. It reads words of two or more Latin letters, apostrophes inside ("don't"), and skips code, the words a suggestion removes, and any stretch with a digit, "@", "/", "\", "_", or a dot between letters (an address, a number, a file name) (`lib/spell-words.ts`). A paragraph that does not read as English — Chinese, Japanese, or Korean text, or four Latin words or more of which fewer than half are English words — gets no squiggle and keeps the browser's own check (`spellcheck="true"` on it); the page has the browser's check off everywhere else, so two underlines never draw. A click on a red word opens its card: up to five spelling suggestions (two neighboring letters swapped first, then those that keep the first letter), Add to dictionary, and Ignore all; a click on a spelling suggestion puts it in the word's place with the word's marks, one undo step (in Suggesting mode, the reader's own suggestion). The right-click menu offers the same. **Add to dictionary and Ignore all** (the right-click menu on a misspelled English word, under its spelling suggestions; a word with no suggestion gets both rows too): Add to dictionary puts the word in the personal dictionary, which holds for every document in this browser (`typingPrefs().dictionary`); Ignore all puts it with the words ignored in this document (`ignoredWords`, by document id, the newest 200 documents kept). The spelling check never flags them: no red squiggle, the menu offers nothing for them, the next and previous misspelling pass them, and the page turns the browser's own check off where each one stands (`spellcheck="false"`, `spellingExceptions` in `typing/spelling.ts`), so no underline draws there either. **Personal dictionary** (Tools > Spelling and grammar, Search the menus; `typing/dictionary-dialog.tsx`): the words A to Z, each with its remove button, and a field that adds one; a change holds at once. Ctrl+' and Ctrl+; select the next and the previous misspelled English word from the selection's start and scroll it into view; a toast says when there is none ("No next misspelling"). Dictionary (Tools, Ctrl+Shift+Y) opens Explain on the selected words, or on the word at the caret.
- **Grammar suggestions** (`typing/proofing.ts`, `components/proofing/grammar-queue.ts`, `/api/grammar`, `lib/prompts/grammar.ts`). A blue squiggle marks the grammar and wording problems of English paragraphs, in Editing and Suggesting (none in Viewing), on Unitos Premium and Ultra. About 2 seconds after the reader stops typing in a paragraph (`GRAMMAR_PAUSE_MS`), the English paragraphs whose text changed go to the model, before the paragraphs waiting; when the document opens in Editing, every English paragraph of three words or more goes too, the paragraphs on screen first, six at a time, one request at a time, at least 0.8 s apart. The model (GLM 5.3 Flash at low effort, the feature `grammar`) answers each paragraph's issues as JSON: the exact wrong words, the words that replace them, and why in a few plain words, in the reader's language; the route Zod-checks the answer and drops every issue whose wrong words are not in the paragraph exactly, and the page drops it again against the paragraph's current text. The answers are kept in the browser by the paragraph's text (the newest 3,000), so a paragraph whose text is unchanged is never sent again. A click on a blue squiggle opens its card, the spelling card's shape: the replacement as a chip (a removal reads Remove “…”), the wrong words struck, and Ignore at the end of that row, the reason under it. A press on the chip accepts: it puts the replacement in place as one edit in Editing, and as the reader's own suggestion in Suggesting mode; Ignore hides that issue for that paragraph's text. Nothing changes the text without a click. Offline nothing is sent; an expired account, a signed-out page, or no model configured stops the check until the page reloads. A grammar suggestion is never a bare "suggestion", which is Suggesting mode's.
- **Navigation keys** (`typing/navigate.ts`), Google Docs' chords: hold Ctrl+Alt (Ctrl+⌘ on a Mac), press a key, then a second key within a second; letting go of a held key cancels the chord, and a character typed with AltGr is never taken. A chord's first key takes the next key, its chord's or any other, so Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T then D never switches to Viewing and E then F never inserts a footnote; Google's first keys whose chords Unitos lacks (A, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+E, Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T) take it too. N (next) or P (previous), then H (a heading or the Title), 1–6 (a heading of that level), G (an image, selected), O (a list), I (an item of the list the caret is in), L (a link), B (a bookmark), F (a footnote number), T (a table; Google's Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N or P, then T, too), C (a comment), U (a suggestion; its card opens), or W (a formatting change: words whose marks, suggestions aside, differ from the words before them) puts the caret at its start and scrolls it into view; a toast says when there is none ("No next heading"). E then C opens the card of the comment around the caret, and the card takes the keys (R, J, K, E, U); E then F puts the caret at the start of the footnote whose number stands at the caret. U then A is Select none: the selection collapses to where the caret stands. Every command whose shortcut is a chord runs from the same reader: O then H or F (the header or the footer), O then U (Review suggested edits), Ctrl+Alt+Shift+W then M (Minimize comments). The keyboard shortcuts dialog lists the chords under Navigation, Comments, Tools, and Editing, written "Ctrl+Alt+N H" ("⌘+Ctrl+N H" on a Mac).

- **Links** (`components/docs/link-dialog.tsx`, `insert/links.ts`). Insert link (Ctrl+K) opens a box under the selection: "Search or paste a link" and Apply. A Text field shows above it when no words are selected or a link is edited. The field takes the keys at once, and the box takes the place of the selection toolbar, which closes. Escape or a press outside closes the box. Keyboard focus in the box is a 2 px clay ring, and Tab stays in the box. "example.com" gains https://, an email mailto:. The typed words, or the selected ones, list the project's documents and this document's headings that match. Headings and bookmarks lists every heading and bookmark. A link to a heading is `#heading=<blockId>`, to a bookmark `#bookmark=<id>`; opening one, or the document's address with one, scrolls to the heading or the bookmark and puts the caret there. A link to a project document opens it in this tab. A caret in a link shows its bubble: the title (the document's, the heading's, or the address), the site, Copy link, Edit link, Remove link. A pasted address of a project document offers Replace URL: Chip or Link. Alt+Enter and Ctrl+click open the link.
- **The "@" menu** (`components/docs/insert/at-menu.tsx`, `at-plugin.ts`), Google Docs' insert menu. "@" at a line's start, or after a space, a tab, "(" or "[", opens it under the "@"; the "@" and the words after it stay in the text as the query, and a gray (sand-500) "Search menu" shows until the first letter. An empty line with the caret reads "Type @ to insert" (sand-600); a press on it opens the menu. Sections: People (the project's collaborators; "@me"), Smart chips, Building blocks, Files, Lists, Media, Headings, Tables, Page components, More; Dates and Emojis join while searching. Page components run the page's commands: Page numbers (its dialog), Page count (off: it goes in a header or footer, from Search the menus), Header and Footer (edited in place), and Page break; a pageless document has them all off. The words filter every section, in bold where they match; a section shows a few rows and › opens all of them. ↑/↓ move, Enter or Tab chooses, → and ← open and leave a section, Escape closes and keeps the words. A choice replaces the "@" and its words with the item, or opens its picker there: a date, a dropdown, the table grid, emoji, image sources, the table of contents' styles, a code block's language. ":" and letters open the menu with emoji only, unless Preferences turns off Insert emojis using the colon character.
- **Smart chips** (`insert/chips.ts`, `insert/chip-cards.tsx`): a date, a person, a project document, or a dropdown, each an atom that draws its label. A date is a calendar day in Google Docs' four formats, with or without a time, in the language of the person who inserted it. Resting on a chip or selecting it shows its card: a date's day, how far it is from today, and its formats; a person's badge; a document's title, which opens it. A press on a dropdown chip lists its options; the Dropdown options dialog saves onto every chip of that dropdown. Building blocks: Meeting notes, Email draft, Product roadmap, Review tracker, Task tracker.
- **The right-click menu** (`insert/context-menu.tsx`), drawn with the toolbar's menus: Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste without formatting, Delete (with Markdown on, Copy as Markdown and Paste from Markdown too); the object's items (an image's, a table of contents', a table's); Comment, Suggest edits (in Editing mode: the page switches to Suggesting), Add to notes, Explain, Ask the assistant — the Unitos tools, which open on the selection as from the selection toolbar (the `docs:unitos-tool` event); Open, Edit, and Remove link, or Insert link; Copy heading link on a heading; Restart numbering and Continue previous numbering on a line of a numbered list; Format options ▸ (Select all matching text, Update 'style' to match), then Clear formatting. The menu opens at once. On a misspelled English word, while Show spelling suggestions is on, up to five spelling suggestions and a separator head it; the first time, they join it when the dictionary has loaded. A click puts the spelling suggestion in the word's place, with the word's marks, one undo step. Shift+right-click keeps the browser's menu (with the browser's spelling suggestions only in a paragraph that does not read as English, where its check stays on). Shift+F10 (Alt+F10 and Shift+F10 go to the toolbox instead while it is open on a selection), Ctrl+Shift+X, and Ctrl+Shift+\ open this menu from the keys, at the caret, with the same spelling suggestions for the word at the caret, the first one highlighted, so Enter fixes the word; while it is open the text's keys stay in it, so the Unitos toolbar never opens beside it; Tab closes it and types nothing. When the browser keeps the clipboard, a dialog names Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, and Ctrl+V.
- **Tables** (`insert/table.ts`, `insert/table-controls.tsx`): inserted from the grid, up to 20 × 20. A cell stores its background, each side's border ("1 solid #000000"), its vertical alignment, and its padding; a row its minimum height and whether it is a pinned header row; the table its alignment and indent. Hover pills drag, pin, sort, and insert rows and columns — the column's pill on the table's top border, in the column's middle, so the line above the table stays readable, and the row's pill left of the table, never past the pane's edge (on a phone it comes over the table); a row's bottom line drags its height. The ▾ at the top right of the caret's cell (or the selected cells) picks which borders change — all, inner, outer, or one line — and draws them in clay until the selection moves; its buttons and the toolbar's then change only those. Colors come from the toolbar's color menu, custom colors included. Split cell asks for columns and rows. Table options is the side panel. Its lengths are in inches (centimeters in Chinese), as in Image options. Sorting keeps pinned rows on top. Header cells (a web page's `th`) are bold on a faint tint of the ink; a PDF's and a Word file's header cells are as the page draws them, their words' weight and fill from the parse. A pageless table fills the text column, its column widths as shares.
- **Images** (`insert/image.ts`, `insert/image-controls.tsx`): a new image keeps its ratio and fits the text's width, and its alt text starts empty. Selected, it shows the clay frame, eight handles, and the rotation handle; a double press crops. The toolbar under it: the five layouts (a page drawn pageless takes In line only), the margin, Crop, Mask image, border color, weight, and dash, Replace image, Reset image, More. In a window under 620 px (a phone) it folds to fit: the five layouts are one Text wrapping menu that shows the current one, the Assistant keeps its symbol, and Replace image and Reset image go to the top of More. **Mask image** (the image toolbar): the image drawn inside a shape — Rectangle (none), Rounded rectangle, Oval, Triangle, Diamond, Pentagon, Hexagon, Star, or Heart (`mask`, lib/docs/mask.ts) — stretched to the image's box; the border follows the shape; Reset image takes it off; the Word download clips the image to the shape. The Image options panel: Size & rotation, Text wrapping, Recolor, Adjustments, Alt text (Ctrl+Alt+Y); closing it gives the page the keys back, the image still selected. Google Docs' keys resize and turn the image. An In line, Wrap text, or Break text image drags to a new place: a drop caret shows where it lands, the toolbar steps aside while it moves, and the image stays selected. A Behind text or In front of text image moves by its own drag, and the arrows move it a quarter inch (a centimeter in Chinese), Shift+arrow one pixel; on any other image an arrow returns the caret to the text. An image pasted, dropped, chosen, or given by URL goes in through one path (`insertImage`, `insertImageFiles`, `insertImageUrls`, `typing/paste.ts`), on its own line after the paragraph that holds the caret (or the drop point), before it when a dropped image's caret stands at its start, or in place of an empty line; one pasted over a selection replaces it. The images' rules refuse a file before anything uploads, with the reason: not png, jpg, gif, webp, or bmp; larger than 25 MB; larger than 5 MB without Unitos Premium (`refuseImage`, lib/images.ts). A file shows at once, faint with a turning ring until it is stored, uploads, and takes the stored address, not as an undo step of its own; one the server refuses goes, with the reason. An image dragged from another page shows from its own address and takes the stored one when the browser may read it. The caret then stands on the empty line under the image, a new line when the next one holds words, so the next key never replaces a new image; a pasted image (a copied one, or pasted words that end with one) leaves the caret the same way.
- **Insert > Chart** (Search the menus: Column chart, Bar chart, Line chart, Pie chart; a table's right-click menu: Chart from this table; `insert/chart-dialog.tsx`, `lib/docs/chart.ts`): the dialog takes the type, the chart title, and the data — a label and a value per series on each row, the series named in the header, at most 50 rows and 8 series — and draws the chart beside the form as it changes. A new chart made with the caret in a table starts from the table (the first column the labels, the first row the series when its cells are words) and lands under the table; elsewhere it starts from three rows to type over and lands at the caret. Insert stores the drawing as a PNG (`POST /api/images`; 600 × 371 px, drawn at twice the size) and the image keeps the data (`chart`, JSON the save checks with `chartSchema`); the alt text is the type and the chart title. A double-click on the chart, Edit chart (the image's bar, its right-click menu, Search the menus) opens the data again, and Update draws the chart anew in its place; Replace image makes it a plain image. A pie chart shows the first series. The Word download and print carry the chart as its image.
- **Insert > Drawing** (Search the menus; `insert/drawing-dialog.tsx`, `lib/docs/drawing.ts`): Google Docs' drawing dialog in Unitos's colors — Undo, Redo, Select, Line, Arrow, Rectangle, Oval, Text box, Scribble, and Delete, then the line color, the fill color (or No fill), the line weight, and the text size, each setting the selected shape and the next ones — over a white canvas. Select moves a shape by a drag, resizes a rectangle or an oval by its corners, and moves a line's ends; Delete removes the selected shape; Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y undo and redo; Escape in a text box ends it and keeps the dialog open, and a text box left empty goes. A shape just drawn is selected, the tool back on Select. Save and close stores the drawing cut to what is drawn, on white, as a PNG (`POST /api/images`, at twice its size), placed at the caret at most 624 px wide; the image keeps the shapes (`drawing`, JSON the save checks with `drawingSchema`: at most 300 shapes and 20,000 scribble points), and its alt text is "Drawing". A double-click on the drawing, or Edit drawing (the image's bar, its right-click menu, Search the menus), opens the shapes again; Replace image makes it a plain image.
- **Insert > Watermark** (Search the menus; `page/watermark-dialog.tsx`, `page/watermark.tsx`, `lib/docs/watermark.ts`): words or an image behind the text of every page, centered on the page (`PageSetup.watermark`). The dialog has a Text tab (the words, font, size, bold, italic, text color, opacity, Diagonal or Horizontal) and an Image tab (an image from the computer, Scale Auto, 50%, 100%, 150%, or 200%, Faded), with a small page that shows the result; Remove watermark takes it off. Pageless and the view without print layout show none; print shows it. The Word download puts it behind the text in each header, centered on the page: the words as the image the dialog drew of them, an image faded when Faded is on.
- **More objects**: footnotes (Ctrl+Alt+F: the number is drawn as a plain superscript in the text's color, and a click beside it puts the caret there, never selecting it; the words stand at the foot of the number's page, or at the document's end in pageless), a horizontal line and a page break (never selected by a click or an arrow, so the next key never takes them), bookmarks (a clay ribbon), equations (KaTeX, typed in the equation box with the five symbol menus, drawn at their paragraph's size, a display's number at the left margin where the page sets it there, `leqno`; the closing marks right after an inline equation — a period, a comma, a bracket, a quote — stay on the equation's line: the equation takes their room as its margin and they give it back, so neither moves; `insert/math.ts`), a table of contents (plain, dotted, or links, with Update and an options panel for the style and the heading levels; each page number is the page its heading stands on), special characters (the dialog stays open), emoji (a picker of nine groups), and Select all matching text (one selection of many ranges).
- **Footnotes** (`insert/footnotes.ts`) are a part of the document apart from the body, as in Google Docs. Ctrl+Home and Ctrl+End (⌘↑ and ⌘↓ on a Mac; Shift extends the selection) go to the start and the end of the body, or of the footnote the caret is in; → and ↓ stop at the end of that part, and ↓ on its last line goes to the line's end; after Select all they put the caret at the body's end. The body ends on a line: a table or another object before the footnotes gets an empty line after it. A cut number takes its footnote along, and a copied number gets its own copy of the footnote (the words travel within one browser tab; a copy of the whole document carries them anywhere). A number pasted into a footnote goes. Footnote words cut, copied, or dragged go in as words wherever they land.

- **Word count** (Ctrl+Shift+C, in Viewing too; `components/docs/word-count.tsx`): Pages, Words, Characters, and Characters excluding spaces, of the body, and of the selection out of the whole when text is selected, with OK alone (it only shows numbers; its boxes take effect at once). It opens with the focus on OK, so Enter closes it, as every dialog with no form and nothing to type in does (Details too). Include headers, footers and footnotes (a checkbox, off each time the dialog opens) counts those too. A word is a run of word characters; apostrophes, hyphens, and dashes never split one, punctuation does, and a CJK run counts as one. "Display word count while typing" keeps a counter at the bottom left of the page area ("N words", "S words out of T"). The toolbar's dialogs are Unitos's dialogs: 24 px corners, the title in Caprasimo, 36 px pill buttons (the primary clay, the rest sand), over an ink scrim at 30%. Tab stays in a dialog, as in the link box. A dialog of fields has Cancel and its primary button (OK, Apply, Split, Insert image); Enter in a field presses the primary, and does nothing while it is off. A dialog closes one way on screen: the ✕ shows only on a dialog with no Cancel, and Escape closes every dialog.

- **The Unitos layer.** The reader interactions (reader-interactions.tsx) wrap the editor as they wrap an article, and every tool, card, and note works as on any document. A selection opens the text toolbar (§6): a mouse selection when the button comes up, a keyboard selection once Shift, Ctrl, or Cmd is let go. A caret moved with the keys closes it, typing closes it, and Escape closes it and keeps the selection. The passage is read from the editor's document (`components/docs/layer/anchor.ts`): one segment per paragraph, offsets as the paragraph index counts them; images and equations are left out, and the toolbar says so. Add comment (the toolbar's button, Ctrl+Alt+M) opens the Comment tool on the selection, or on the word the caret stands in, and its field has the focus before the next key; a comment is an annotation. When the comment field closes, posted or cancelled, the page takes the focus back with the caret after the commented words. The marks of notes, annotations, links, and extractions paint over the rich text as decorations (`components/docs/annotation-marks.tsx`), one per stretch of words under the same marks, as the reader draws them: a link wins, else the smallest anchor names the mark. Marks keep the kind colors (§6). A mark's chips (a tool's symbol, a comment's icon — a highlight that holds a comment carries it too —, a plain highlight's dot in its hue, a link's chain, an extraction's label) sit outside the document (`data-anchor-skip`) and take no room in the line: right of the text column (in a table, at the cell's right edge), level with the line the mark ends on, side by side when several end on one line, so a new mark never moves the words. In Viewing a click on a mark or a chip opens what it opens in the reader, also while the selection still holds its words: the caret goes to the click; a click on linked words opens the annotation or note under the link. In Editing and Suggesting the reader is writing: a click on marked words places the caret and opens nothing; a Ctrl+click (⌘ on a Mac) on them, or a click on the mark's chip, opens what the mark opens, and the mark's tooltip says so ("Ctrl+click to view the annotation"). A touch screen has no Ctrl+click: there a tap on a mark opens what it opens in Editing too, a second tap on the same mark or a tap off the marks places the caret, and a tap within 12px of a chip is the chip's, however small the page is drawn. The margin's comment cards hold their place while the selection toolbox is open: the toolbox stands over them. Words under two annotations or notes or more open the reader's chooser at the click, a note among them too (its row opens the note in the tray), as in the block reader; the mark takes the smallest anchor's place (of two alike, an annotation's, then the lower source id) and an annotation's color, never a plain note's clay over a highlight. A jump flashes the words as a decoration. The selection under the toolbar is the page's own (see The caret): an article's clay tint (§6) is not painted on the page, so opening the toolbar never repaints the page. A repaint happens when the highlights change in content or a new stored revision is on screen (someone else's words, whose marks the server moved), never for a re-render — a repaint puts the editor's selection back into the page — and only while the screen holds the stored copy the highlights' offsets were read from (nothing waits to be saved, the page's revision caught up); meanwhile the painted marks move with the typing, and a paragraph moved with Ctrl+Shift+↑ or ↓ (or brought back by Ctrl+Z) keeps its marks. A mark made on this screen and not stored yet (a new comment or highlight) paints at once from its anchor, over the painted marks; the stored mark takes its place with the next repaint. A save that lost a mark or found one again asks for the stored highlights at once. Typing saves before an anchor is stored, a plan runs, a dragged passage lands, or the voice command reads the document (`components/docs/layer/flush.ts`). A passage dragged onto a note in the notes tray lands in it as a quote (§6); dragged inside the page it moves. In Editing and Suggesting, a press outside the page (the notes tray) takes the browser's selection out of the page while the page still draws it; a press on the drawn selection puts it back first, so the drag carries the quote there as in Viewing, and a click without a move puts the caret. A blank document has no reading mode and no block edit mode, no Contents menu, in a split pane too. Extract is in the side panel's Extract tab, where it is for every document; the toolbar's right end holds the status, the version clock, Collapse, and the mode. A viewer sees the page and the toolbar with the editing controls off.
- **The margin** (`components/docs/layer/margin.ts`), as in Google Docs. The toolbar and the cards stand in one column beside the page (`CardColumn`, `layer/comment-card.tsx`): a fixed layer over the pane, under the title row and the toolbar, that scrolls with the pane; `suggest/layer.tsx` places the cards in it. The toolbar sits 8 px right of the page's right edge, its first row level with the selection's first line, and fades in over 250 ms. With no room there it sits over the page's right margin, never over the text. The comment's field and the assistant's box widen it from 176 to 248 px: when the wider toolbar would cover the text, the page moves left for it as for a card; with no room even then, the toolbar keeps 176 px. Cards are 282 px wide (260 at the least), 20 px corners, sand-100 at rest, sand-200 under the pointer, the card color with the float shadow while in use; the border keeps the kind color. The cards keep the order of their words, each level with its words or pushed clear of its neighbors: the open card level with its words, the cards above it moved up, the cards below it moved down, none above the page's top. The open card stays in reach: pushed past the pane's bottom by a card that stays where it is, it goes above that card, inside the pane; and the cards under it whose words are in view end inside the pane too, the open card going up with them while it still stands beside its words. The toolbar and the tools' cards stay where they are, and the cards flow around them the same way: the cards whose words come before the selection go above the toolbar, the others below it, so the toolbar never covers a card. The page moves left to make room (`--docs-shift` on the pane) and moves back once the column holds no card and no toolbar is open, never under a held press. A log card never moves the page. With no room for the column beside the page even with the page at the canvas's left edge (1440 px with the notes tray open), the column stands over the page's right margin, never over the page's text. The toolbar and the cards stay inside the pane: they never cover the notes tray, whatever tab it shows, or its rail. With no room at all (1280 px with the notes tray open, a pageless import at 1440 px with the notes tray open, a high zoom, the contents panel open), the toolbar opens under the words and only the open card shows, under its words, drawn as the one-line card with Accept and Reject at its end (its time in the tooltip), so it covers one line of the words and not four; a comment the reader has just made opens its card there once it is stored, so the new comment's card shows. In a split pane there is no column: the comments show as their icons, and only the open card shows, under its words, as one line. A wheel over a card scrolls the page, and the pane reaches down to the lowest card. The title row and the toolbar stay above the cards. A card at rest opens on the press, not on the release: the press closes the open card and the cards move, and the pressed card still opens. Beside the page, Add to notes' list of sections drops down over the tools, so it never goes under the header.
- **Comments** (`components/docs/layer/comment-card.tsx`), Google Docs' comment card. Every comment has its card in the column (The margin): at rest one line, the author and the comment's first words. A click on the comment's words or its icon, or a press on its line, opens the whole card as the card in use: lifted, with Resolve (✓), Delete (a trash icon, as on the block reader's comment card), and More options (⋯: Edit, Get link to this comment) shown, while the caret stays in the page; on a coarse pointer the three icons are 36 px. A press on the card's text leaves the caret in the page: typing goes to the page, never to the card. It shows the author's badge (32 px), name, and time, then the comment; without sign-in the author is the local reader, named "You" as the history names them. The comment's replies (§12) sit under it, with Reply. Resolve (✓, E on the focused card) is on every comment's card for an editor, with sign-in on or off. It resolves the comment (`Note.resolvedById`; `PATCH /api/annotations/[noteId] {resolved}`, editor): the card closes, the mark and its icon go, and the page takes the keys again. A resolved comment paints no mark, so it opens no card, on every document alike. It lists under Resolved in the Annotations tab and on the annotations full page, with its replies as they were, and Reopen in its ⋯ menu paints it again. Edit, or a click on the comment's words, turns the comment into a field with Cancel and Save (Ctrl+Enter saves, Escape cancels). Get link to this comment copies the comment's address in the reader (§6); the address opens the page on the card. The card reads its author, time, and replies from the note's routes (`GET /api/notes/[noteId]/edits`, `GET /api/replies?noteId=`). On the focused card, Google's keys: R reply, J the next comment, K the previous one, E resolve, U back to the text (a card has the focus only when J or K, Tab, or a press on one of its buttons took it there, never while the caret is in the page); the keyboard shortcuts dialog lists them under Comments. A press anywhere else, or Escape, closes the card.
- **View > Comments** (Search the menus). Show all comments (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+A, or hold Ctrl+Alt+Shift and press W then E) opens the Annotations tab (the `dissect:show-annotations` event), the list of every comment with its replies, the resolved ones under Resolved. Minimize comments (hold Ctrl+Alt+Shift, press W then M) takes the comments' cards out of the column and leaves each comment its icon at the end of its words; a click on the icon opens its card. Hide comments (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+J) unpaints the comments and their icons until Minimize comments, Show all comments, or a new comment. Viewing mode hides the comments; Editing shows them again.
- **Suggesting mode** (`components/docs/ext/suggest.ts`, `components/docs/suggest/`), Google Docs' own, on `@handlewithcare/prosemirror-suggest-changes`. The mode menu has Editing, Suggesting ("Edits become suggestions"), and Viewing; Ctrl+Alt+Shift+X switches to Suggesting, and the pill turns clay. Suggesting is for editors: a viewer cannot suggest. In Suggesting mode every edit becomes a suggestion, and the whole toolbar stays: added words draw in the author's person color, underlined on a light tint of it; removed words stay, struck through in that color; a format change (a mark on words; a paragraph's style, alignment, spacing, or indent) takes effect and draws a dotted line; a suggested paragraph break shows a ¶. Blocks a suggestion puts back with the same words (a paragraph or a table's row moved, a list toggled) draw once: the removed copy hides. A table's column moved draws at both places, struck at the old one: its cells hidden would leave their column empty, and the table would draw narrower. A suggestion is the `insertion`, `deletion`, and `modification` marks under one id, `<account id>.<ms>` (`suggestionAuthor`, `suggestionTime` in `lib/docs/schema.ts`, beside the mark names and the zero-width space that holds a suggested break), so it names its author and time with nothing more stored; words typed next to another person's suggestion get their own id. Backspace, Delete, or typing over another person's added words strikes them as this person's deletion, drawn over their added words with a card of its own (Delete: "…"); in one's own added words they go outright. Delete strikes the letter after the caret and the caret moves past it, press after press, as Backspace strikes the letter before it. Backspace or Delete on a suggested paragraph break takes the break back: the lines join, and Enter then Backspace leaves nothing behind. A command that puts the caret in what it adds (a table's first cell, a footnote, a new row, a new list line) puts it there in Suggesting mode too. A change of letter case is one suggestion: the stretch it changes in each paragraph, replaced. A block's id is no formatting: its change is never a suggestion. A paragraph style changed in Suggesting mode keeps the block's id and its other attributes (alignment, spacing); it records the block's own value before the change, and every attribute the new style lacks (a heading's level, a title's style), so Reject puts the style back whole. The rich text saves the marks (`lib/docs/schema.ts` keeps a suggestion's id and checks a format change's values as the mark, the attribute, or the node type that rejecting it puts back). The paragraph index reads the text as if every person's suggestion were accepted and every one of the assistant's rejected (The paragraph index), so notes, Explain, search, and Stitch read "Bring the slides", never "Bring the reportslides"; the editor's anchors count the same way, a mark stops short of struck words at its ends, a selection only in a person's struck words or in the assistant's added words gives no quote, and a style from the assistant leaves removed words as they are. Clear formatting, Paint format, and Repeat leave the suggestion marks alone. Every suggestion has its card in the column beside the page, the comment card's look, among the comments' cards in the order of their words (The margin). The card of the suggestion the caret stands in is whole, white and lifted, and level with its words; the cards above it move up and the cards below it move down: the author's badge, name, and time; Add: "…", Delete: "…", Replace: "…" with "…", Move: "…", or Format: …; and, for an editor, Accept suggestion (✓) and Reject suggestion (✕), each one undo step. Every other card is one line, the author and the change; a press on it selects the suggestion and opens its card. With no room at all, and in a split pane, only the open card shows, under its words. A card reads a line break as ↵, a paragraph break as ¶, and a checklist tick as Format: Checked or Unchecked. A suggestion that takes whole blocks out and puts the same words back draws them once, the removed copy hidden: a list changed where its words stand (a list toggled, a bullet removed, bullets made numbers, a line nested) reads Format: Bulleted list, Numbered list, Checklist, Indent, or remove bulleted list, and draws on the list's bullets, numbers, and checklist boxes in the author's color, as Google Docs draws it (a line that left its list draws the dotted line; a list line added or removed whole keeps its bullet or number); blocks moved (Ctrl+Shift+↑/↓) read Move: "…". A list change made in more than one step (a numbered line made a checklist, a line joined to the list above it) is one such suggestion all the same, and a format change on its words stays with them. A list edit over the author's own list change (a list toggled again, a line of the new list nested, Select all then another list) takes its place: one list change from the text before both, so Accept gives the last list and Reject gives the text before both; a list put back as it was is no suggestion.
